**The person-behavior questions.** `Question` also carries five decisions the cabinet's own people take, implemented through the shared willingness pipeline: `certify-order` (the Attorney General clearing an executive order or objecting in writing; weighs `overreach`, `principle`, `deference`, `refusal-record`, `statute`), `carry-out` (the chair who runs an act the President ordered carrying it out at pace or dragging it; `deference`, `policy-fit`, `times-overruled`, `department-exposure`, `boldness`), `aim-leak` (a leaking minister aiming the leak at a colleague; `colleague-enmity`, `deference`), `minister-initiative` (a minister bringing an act of their own; `loyalty`, `ambition`, `times-overruled`, `policy-fit`, `co-partisanship`) and `steer-contracts` (a chair steering contracts; `principle`, `ambition`, `budget-reach`, `oversight`). Three existing questions may weigh new terms: `receive-engagement` and `accept-treaty` gain `envoy` (the Secretary of State's craft) and `rapport` (how the two leaders get on), and `lobbied-member` gains `advocate` (the Secretary who made the case). `npc-act` does not gain `rapport`: rapport reaches a foreign government's own move through how it reads the other side, which `intent-match` already names, so a term of its own would weigh one reading twice. Every new key has its phrase in `reason-lines.ts`; a question that reads a shared key the other way round gets its own phrasing there (`certify-order`'s `principle`), and keys sized by a never-negative quantity (`times-overruled`, `department-exposure`) name the quantity both ways.

**What the person-behavior questions do.** Each is a willingness question with its stamp on the packet it changes, its forecast inside the existing projection, and its reason on the screen where the act is taken.
- `certify-order` (`order-review.ts`): the seated or acting Attorney General reviews any order whose reach is 0.15 or more, inside `projectOrder` (`OrderProjection.review`), threshold −0.45, no noise. An objection multiplies the challenge chance by 1.30; signing anyway writes `counsel {AG, "against"}`, so the existing overruled-loyalty and break machinery applies. A clearance writes no counsel. The order page says which before the President signs.
- `carry-out` (`execution.ts`): the chair who runs an act — from the US order table (`orderDepartment`/`orderReviewer` in `department-ownership.ts`, `orderChair` in `orders.ts`: each order belongs to the department whose statute it runs on, and the Attorney General reviews every order), `ministerFor` for instruments, the institution's chair for directives — carries it out at pace or drags it. Threshold 0, no noise; `delayShare` grades the drag. An operation's added days are inside `actionTiming` (`ActionTiming.pace`), so the card and the due date agree; `delayedDays` converts a drag and a speedup to whole days the same way, at least a day either way and at most half the operation's book time, so a bold Secretary is never rounded off a short operation; a rule's `effectiveTurn` can run up to six weeks past `RULEMAKING_TURNS`; a directive is slow-walked at a share of 0.5. Only boldness speeds a military operation. Statutory refusals do not move.
- `aim-leak` (`cabinet-revolt.ts`): a leaking Secretary aims the leak at a colleague only when their tie is opposed **and** their record in the room is negative (a lost argument is a reason to leak about someone; a feud on paper is not). An aimed leak costs half the standing, is tagged `rival`, and its scandal names the target. The number of leaks is unchanged by aiming.
- `minister-initiative` (`cabinet-initiative.ts`, `cabinet-week.ts`, `cabinet-agenda-acts.ts`): a Secretary brings an act matching who they are — Defense a posture notch where its force appetite leans, State a summit or visit the other side would receive, an order-owning chair its best-fitting order within its principle and boldness — through the existing one-a-week triage, with a half-year cooldown per Secretary. Taking it dispatches to `signOrder`, `setCommandPosture` or `engagementPacket` and pays the act's own price.
- `envoy` and `rapport` (`engagement.ts`, `treaty-counterpart.ts`): the Secretary of State's bench-read craft scales the warmth a received President's envoy, visit, summit or declaration buys (×0.88–1.12 across the bench, 0.85 with the chair empty, inside `warmthEffects` and so inside `projectEngagement.effects`) and is a small term on receiving and on treaty acceptance; rapport between the President and the other leader is a term on both, hidden for thin records. Applied to the President's own requests only.
- `advocate` (`lobbying-advocate.ts`): the Secretary whose department owns a bill's committee backs the Hill call or negotiation, `0.12 × craft lean` (×0.6 across the aisle), inside the lobbying projection.

**Foreign leaders move their governments.** `liveTemperament` is the nation's traits plus, within ±0.1 together, the stored seat offset and the incumbent's residual (`incumbentResidual`, `leaderLean` in `foreign-leader.ts`): how far the seated leader sits from the leader that kind of government usually has. So the leader in office at the start now moves their country too, and a successor reads against the same reference. `personalLean` fills opportunism (ambitious +0.04, cautious −0.04) and vengefulness (pragmatic −0.04); transparency stays 0. In `npc-agent.ts`, hostility toward another capital falls by `0.08 × rapport` between the two leaders, via `leader-index.ts` (the cast sorted by nationality once per cast object). Measured over 5 seeds × 104 weeks the NPC world held: actions +0.6%, the share aimed at the President unchanged, median NPC wars unchanged.

**Leaf modules against the import cycle.** The instrument ladder sits on a value-import cycle with modules that time, price and staff an act. A reading those modules call at use time must not pull the ladder in half-built at load, so the instinct reading lives in `counsel-instinct.ts`, the coercion table in `instrument-ids.ts`, the overruled count in `counsel-stance.ts`, and `wire.ts` builds its rung maps on first use. `module-load-order.test.ts` loads fourteen engine entry modules first in a fresh graph.

**The engine's import cycles, and the rule that keeps them loadable.** `conflicts.ts` imports `war-tick.ts`, which imports `sideOf` from `conflicts.ts`, `allyJoinEffects` from `war-join.ts` and `alliancePackets` from `peace.ts`, and `war-join.ts` imports `sideOf` back; `war-join.ts` also imports `compacts.ts`, `commitments.ts`, `relationships.ts`, `warfighting.ts` and `war-entry.ts`, and `conflicts.ts#assessDeterrence` calls back into it. That is safe only because none of them reads an import at module scope. Three module-scope reads once broke the built engine's load: `governance.ts` computed its catalogue scale from `policy.ts`'s `POLICY_STANCES` at load, and `policy.ts` reaches `governance.ts` back through `demos.ts → opinion.ts → household-prices.ts → commodities.ts` (now computed on first use); `monetary.ts` used to re-export `monetary-acts.ts`, which read `FED_CREDIBILITY_PREMIUM` at load (now read at call time); and `effects.ts` imported the four world effect handlers from their phases, dragging `markets.ts` and `foreign-politics.ts` (both of which import `governance.ts`) into the effect module's cycle (the handlers now live in the leaf `world-effects.ts`). `war-join.ts` must not import `governance.ts`; its regime-danger reading is restated and pinned by a test. A module that reads another module's export at load time is the failure this rule exists to prevent; after any import change, load every built module as an entry point. No module inside a cycle re-exports another module: a re-export is a load-time read, so importers name the source module, and the engine index exports it.

**What fills the stubs.** War outcomes are filled: `settlementEffects`, `casualtyBand`, `munitionsPhase` and `selectMunitions`, `selectBases`, `declaredWarheads` (Israel undeclared), and `openingWorld`, which draws the seeded board or the `world-2026` preset with its insurgencies, installations and sanctions; `selectInheritedWorld` is the reading of what the President inherits. A spent US munitions class refuses the ladder rungs it supplies (rungs 3 to 5, `munitionsShortfall`), as it already refused the join; nothing but a war the United States fights draws US stocks. Alliance decisions: the join, the call and peace, as above. Foreign politics and press: `foreign-politics.ts` holds `foreignPoliticsPhase` (per nation, at most one change a week, checked in this order: a death in office; a scheduled selection whose `next` date has come, with its result drawn from `stream("foreign-election", nation, date)` against `incumbentStanding`; a confidence vote in a parliamentary multiparty government, drawn from the same key's `"confidence"` sub-stream at `confidenceRate`, after a 182-day mandate grace and outside the last 91 days before an election, calling a snap election 42 days out; a coup weighed as `"coup-hazard"`, whose score is the weekly chance, stamped `intelligence`; then the week of the press abroad), `interregnum` (the 79 pre-oath selections resolved at the seal by the same rule, seeded, provenance `projected`), and `succeedLeader`/`succeedAfterDefeat`. Every change seats through `seatingEffects` (the person's `currentRole`, which `foreignLeaderFor` ranks first), writes the `leadership` effect and a `governance` feed item, and, where the governing party changes, the new government's program (`governance.ts#programOfNewGovernment`). `press-foreign.ts` holds eight fictional outlets and the coverage (`coverageScore`: state media below `STATE_PRESS_CEILING` echo their government's trust; a free press weighs the President's politics, half the ticket's platform and half the held program, against the governing party, by its freedom) and `pressTrustAffinity`/`coverageWeek`. `article-foreign.ts` and `article-leadership.ts` hold the banks. A foreign head of government's traits read the government's appetite in the same draws (`foreign-leader.ts#pickLeadTraits`), and their lean (`personalLean`, `leaderOffset`) is what `liveTemperament` applies. **Engagement** (`engagement.ts`): call, envoy, state visit, summit, joint declaration; one priced path (`projectEngagement` → `engagementPacket`) for the President and every government; the receiving government weighs `receive-engagement`; a received engagement moves both edges by at most 0.06; a kind waits out a per-kind cooldown read from the edge's history. The NPC agent scores engagements beside its coercive candidates in one argmax, without the ripple walk, at 0.85 weight. **Cause to coerce** (`propensity.ts#coercionCause`): an NPC's coercive candidates need a sourced rivalry, a crossing, a grudge, a hostile standing, a US trade measure, a past war or a doctrine-named target; otherwise it weighs only assistance and engagement. **Presidency exposure** (`npc-agent.ts#presidencyExposure`): removal pressure cheapens and standing dears a move against the President, 0.7-1.3, multiplied into the revealed-preference price. **World events** (`worldEventPhase`, day 3): sanctions package (customs-union members vote `org-vote`, 85% supermajority), border clash (`world-event`; never opens a war; a willing pair clashes on a weekly hazard `1 − exp(−λ)` drawn per pair and week, a base of 0.01 raised by an episode of recent clashes and shows of force between the pair at a 56-day half-life, zero for 4 weeks after a clash, and damped to a tenth where the side that would be hit sits under a defense treaty the attacker is outside, half that again after a consultation in the past year; each pair draws once a week, and a clash under a guarantee files one consultation per body, `world-event:consultation`, which deters the attacker against the members for a year), accession and withdrawal (`world-membership.ts`, quarterly summits, bodies without the US: leaving is a notice under the body's own exit clause, filed on the ledger as `withdrawal-notice`, decided again at the first summit after it has run and taken back, `withdrawal-notice-revoked`, if the grievance mends; the weighing reads grievance, trade, a defense guarantee's worth and the exit's cost, and a body without an exit clause costs a repudiation), disaster relief (`send-aid`), election interference (`world-event`, intelligence-only filing), and war aid from a government whose join fell short by under 0.35 (`send-aid`, reading `joinCounterfactual`). Every party decides through `weigh`; the only draw is `willDraw`. **Treaty counterpart** (`treaty-counterpart.ts`): `signTreaty` calls `counterpartRefusal` after the administration's own trust floor; `accept-treaty` weighs the counterpart's trust in the US and its live legalism, reliability and isolationism against `ACCEPT_TREATY_THRESHOLDS`. **Customs unions** (`instrument-gates.ts#tariffCompetence`): a customs-union member has no tariff rung; an FTA member cannot tariff a fellow member. **Missile reach** (`missile-reach.ts`): seven sourced ranges; adds force propensity toward capitals in range (and toward the US through countries hosting US forces), and a standoff alternative on the strike rung: a government in range may strike by missile without the air package, and such a strike commits no airframes. Markets (5E): see "Markets and the household bill" below; the debt rebase (`dataset/debt-rebase.ts#applyDebtRebase`) stays the identity until a sourced IMF row is opened. The contest (5K): `opponent.ts#selectOpponent` (the stored opponent, else the seed's draw) and `succession.ts#successionOf` (the Vice President, else the Speaker, once `removal.resolved`); `removal.ts` appends `successionHandover` to the resolved arc's `endingSummary` under an impeachment charter. `section4Succession` answers the Twenty-fifth Amendment's fourth section for the harness only. Player-core `elections/finale.ts` (with `race.ts`, `legacy.ts`, `statehouses.ts`, `finale-copy.ts`) composes the ending, the legacy, the race reading and the election-night count for `/ending` and `/legacy`. Still neutral: player-core `world/feed-rank.ts`, `world/board-panels.ts`, `world/war-file.ts` and `security/pdb.ts`, each listed in `scripts/engine-internal-readings.json` until a screen draws it.

**Two new attention kinds, and what the loop can stop on.** `AttentionSource` gains `"market-shock"` (pauses on arrival) and `"coup"` (pauses on arrival where the President reads that government; a producer logs it otherwise), both in `ATTENTION_SOURCES` and `PAUSE_BY_DEFAULT` with a label in the attention settings. `player-core/desk/attention-markets.ts` raises both: `marketShockItems` the week the index falls 8% (`selectMarketShock`), and `coupItems` from the engine's one reading of a coup (`foreign-politics.ts#coupMatters`), soft where the services read the government and ambient otherwise. Soft rather than hard so the player's attention setting governs it; pausing is only the default. `advanceUntilAttention`'s `AttentionStop` presents either through the desk module's matter source; releases and foreign elections are feed items and never stop the clock.

**The opening board at the seal.** `sealFounding` computes `openingWorld(nations, openingDate, seed, choices.openingPreset)` and, after `foundingConfirmations` and `interregnum`, lays its conflicts, operators, commitments and trade measures onto the campaign (`withOpeningWorld`) before `recomputeApproval`. The sanctions programs in force are filed from `opening-world.ts#INHERITED_PROGRAMS`: each carried program's measures by kind, sector and covered share as the primary text scopes them, dated at the authority's effective date (quoted from the source, and a test parses that date) so evasion reads them as mature; list-based programs file `scope: "listed"`. The seeded board's keep weights still decide which programs are carried. The harness-only `carriedWorld: false` founding option skips it; `foundedForBalance` takes it as a fourth argument, and no player path passes it.

**Security Council.** `security-council.ts` reads the registry's council rule (`ORG-UNSC`, `Organization.council`). `tallyCouncil` applies Charter Art. 27 (nine votes; a permanent "against" on a substantive question vetoes; an abstention does not; a procedural question has no veto). `councilPosition` derives each member's vote from the relationship record, treaty and defense-body ties (planning values: margins by measure, a 0.25 treaty-partner bonus, half weight on the sponsor); `councilEffects` states what an adopted draft does and never grants domestic authority or a military result (DECISIONS 6.21). The roster is not mirrored onto `Nation.memberships`. Exported from the engine; nothing in the loop calls it yet.

**Markets and the household bill.** `commodityPhase` (day 4, before `macroDriftPhase`) is the one writer of `Campaign.markets`. Each of the eleven commodities steps through `commodity-kernel.ts#stepMarket`: supply (1 − the week's shocks + spare output + producers answering thin stocks) against demand (falling with price), stocks moving by the gap, and a partial adjustment toward a target set by the shortfall, the stocks and a route-risk premium; a seeded residual per commodity and week. Shocks are derived every week from what the campaign already holds (`commodity-shocks.ts`): a principal belligerent's lost output, a strait contested by a war with a government on its shores as a party, a blockade on station against a supplier, measures on a supplier or by one, read from the one scoped reach record (`external-pressure.ts#Coercion`: sector and goods, covered share, direction, authority, financial reach) flow by flow through `stoppedShare` (per imposer the heaviest measure, across imposers independent shares of what is left; DECISIONS §6.17). A measure reaches a commodity only where its `CommodityDef.sector` and `good` are in scope. A measure on a supplier takes `severity × SANCTION_BITE` of what it stops: what the target sells the imposer, plus its financial reach of what it sells everyone else, diverted only as far as the goods' substitutability allows. Only the administration's designations and two-way embargoes carry reach (`DOLLAR_REACH` 0.8, less evasion: `EVASION` ceiling 0.5 with 365- and 730-day half-lives off the register's dates); an asset freeze, an import-only embargo, a listed program and another capital's measure carry none, and an export control stops none of the target's sales but costs the target the inputs denied. Sanction and blockade shocks are charged on what the supplier's war left. Each producer and economy is charged only the change since its `MACRO_ANCHORS` growth vintage, so lifting a program already embedded in the founding figures returns supply, acute conditions on a supplier, a reserve release. The straits are the rows of the world's `CHOKEPOINT_FLOWS` table (`commodity-registry.ts#routesFor`): each names its littoral governments, the commodity's share of world supply that passes it and the share a pipeline or a sea detour can carry instead, all read off EIA pages. Both route terms are charged on the flow that cannot go around and was still flowing (`share × (1 − bypass) × stopped × (1 − upstreamLost) × intensity`, times `ROUTE_CLOSURE` 0.68 for lost supply, fitted so a full-intensity Hormuz war takes 9.6% of world supply, April 2026's 10.4 of 108.42 Mb/d, and `ROUTE_RISK` 0.3 for the premium, which is priced on every ship that still has to cross, flags let through included, since war-risk cover is written on the transit). `upstreamLost` is the share of the strait's flow its shippers had already lost to war, blockade or measure, each weighted by supply share × exported share × `geo.chokepointDependence`, so an importer's own production never counts (`commodity-shocks.ts#contestedRoutes`, cached per snapshot); and a strait with no sourced row moves nothing. Today the rows are Hormuz crude, LNG and nitrogen (traded urea, share 0.039, no bypass) and Bab el-Mandeb crude. `stopped` is the share of the strait's cargo the governments closing it will not pass (`strait-passage.ts#straitPassage`): each littoral party to the threatening war lets through cargo bound for itself, its co-belligerents, a government aligned with it either way (`doctrine.alignmentPull`) or one it reads at `PASSAGE_DISPOSITION` (0.3, a planning value) or better, and never its enemies in the war; destinations come from the world's `CHOKEPOINT_CARGO` (Hormuz crude by destination, 1H25), cargo filed under a region is stopped, and a strait with no split stops everything (`stopped` 1). The route shock carries the reading as `passage` and its cause names who is let through; in a war between Iran and the United States Iran passes China's cargo, so 0.634 of Hormuz crude is stopped. Fed net loss rather than the gross drop in transits, crude's kernel is fitted to EIA's weekly Brent over the 2026 closure (`commodity-registry.ts`, `CRUDE`: sensitivity 6, speed 0.8, weekly cap 0.16, against the energy planning values 2.5, 0.35 and 0.12 the gas markets keep; RBRTE weekly from $71.36 the week of 2026-02-27 to a $124.61 peak the week of 2026-04-10): fed EIA's shut-ins it peaks at $118, 13% under the causal change, week 4 sits 6% off March's average and week 8 at April's $117 (`commodity-calibration.test.ts`), an endogenous full-intensity Hormuz war peaks at 1.646 of rest against the weekly 1.746, inside a quarter (`commodity-hormuz.test.ts`), and a full-intensity Bab el-Mandeb war still moves Brent under 5% in twelve weeks. Prices reach economies only through `external-pressure.ts` (`commodity-pressure.ts`: US households on BLS CPI weights through the pump, grocery and power bills with their lags; abroad by wealth tier; growth by the energy balance and export shares, on delivered volume: an exporter earns the price only on what it delivers (less its war's lost output, a blockade and its in-scope measures), loses cargo stranded behind a contested strait at `COERCION_BITE`, and is not stranded by its own closure; an importer whose flag is stopped goes short at `SHORTFALL_COST` 0.1 by its cargo through the strait over its own oil consumption, and an exporter's stranded share is its loadings over its crude production less its own consumption (`NATION_OIL_GAS`, `CHOKEPOINT_CARGO`, `CHOKEPOINT_ORIGINS`; `commodity-shocks.ts#cargoDependence`, `#originDependence`; upstream deduplication weights shippers by the same loadings; LNG and any nation a table does not name fall back to `geo.chokepointDependence`); a non-US pair's export share is world exports in the sector × partner share (`partnerShare`, `tradeDependence / tradeExposure`), and a sector's share of exports is its output mix over the `trade.topExports` sectors (`exportScope`)), shown as the `commodities` macro driver. A blockade stands only while its navy keeps the rung's reach and naval strength (`blockades.ts#blockadeReaches`). A standing blockade of a littoral government by a government with no shore on the strait adds that navy to the passage (`StraitPassage.blockaders`, derived, never stored), stopping cargo bound for the blockaded government and for its own enemies at war, and no one else's (`strait-passage.ts#blockaderLetsThrough`); the blockaded government's own loadings are its blockade supply loss, counted once (DECISIONS 6.10). The Strategic Petroleum Reserve is carried in barrels (`SPR_OPENING_BARRELS`, 282.7 million, EIA week of 2026-10-16): a full drawdown needs the severe-interruption finding, which now names both 42 U.S.C. 6241(d) prongs, and a limited drawdown under 6241(h) adds 30 million barrels over 60 days (0.00485 of world supply), once per shortage and never below 252.4 million (`commodity-levers.ts#releaseLimitedReserve`, DECISIONS 6.20). The United States carries a critical-input channel for semiconductors (`external-pressure.ts#chipShortfall`, `criticalInputCut`): a war or blockade on a supplier of recorded US chip imports (Taiwan 0.233, Malaysia 0.199, Korea 0.094) cuts the output of `CHIP_DEPENDENT_SECTORS` by that supplier's share times what it lost, times one less the substitutability (0.2), under the coercion cap; partners carry no chip channel. Diversion builds with a 91-day half-life (`DIVERSION_HALF_LIFE_DAYS`, fitted to Census goods trade, 2018-2019). The same phase steps the equity index (`markets.ts#nextEquity`) and records the Fed's meeting (`monetary.ts#fedRecord`), whose rate the macro drift sets through `usFedInput` — the rule with the chair's tilt and any public lean. `defaultPhase` weighs governments whose real interest bill has climbed past their rating's margin (`default-hazard`; a real rate below zero is no bill, so inflation eroding a stock never reads as stress), and a default writes a haircut, the letter D, a growth shock and a wire item; `DEFAULT_RERATE_WEEKS` (52, a planning value) after a default this campaign recorded, the government is re-rated `CCC` with its own wire item. A government the dataset opens at D is never re-rated, because it has not restructured on the record. The attention list adds `market-shock` (the week the index falls 8%) and `coup` (soft where the services read the government, logged otherwise); releases are ambient rows on their dates, printing the figure stored when each was issued (`recordReleases`), so an old release never shows today's economy.

**Trade wedges, answers and diversion (world dynamics Wave 2).** On lift, the share of a tariff's step that had passed through by the lift phases back out over the next 26 weeks as negative pressure on the inflation target (`tariff-prices.ts`, `TariffPriceMove.unwinding`), matching the shelf reversal in `trade-effects.ts#liftPriceMove`; a held tariff keeps its wedge, and `measures` still lists only live tariffs. A partner's answering tariff cuts American sales at `RETALIATION_RESPONSE` (6.2, `trade-volume.ts#retaliationFall`, fitted so a 25 percent answer on row crops cuts sales to China by about the 76 percent USDA ERS reports), through the trade book (`retaliationLeft`) and the US growth term (`external-pressure.ts#retaliationCut`, `COERCION_BITE` 0.5 of the lost sales, a planning value-added share unverified against ERS's cash-receipts figure). The odds of that answer are read off the same cut (`trade.ts#retaliationOutlook`: `retaliationFall` on the sector it would hit at four fifths of the rate, `RETALIATION_CUT_WEIGHT` 0.6, a planning value set so the mean odds across founding partners at a 25 percent tariff stay at the rate-read 0.43; measured 0.42), and `TariffIncidence.retaliationCut` carries it for the preview. The partner picks the sector by salience × what it buys × the answer's bite (border pass-through × substitutability, `retaliationTargetFor`), so China answers on row crops, as it did in 2018, rather than on civil aircraft it cannot replace. A measure that cuts a partner's US sales in a sector (tariff, sanctions, freeze or embargo; not export controls or listed programs) frees `lost × substitutability` of them to untouched suppliers, building at `DIVERSION_HALF_LIFE_DAYS` (91, fitted to Census goods trade: about half of China's lost 2018 to 2019 sales to the United States were bought elsewhere within the year), each increment of lost sales from the date of the measure that added it (`trade-volume.ts#lostIncrements`), so a token restriction cannot pre-age a larger later one; the freed sales are shared by recorded US sales and capped by each supplier's export headroom, and total imports stay below the opening.

### Incident impacts and scheduled actions

October 9: foreign action terms share one `ResolutionRequest`, forecast and executor. `action-support.ts` owns explicit unilateral/seek choices, partner response dates and confirmed/refused/conditional answers. `instrument-issued.ts` stores requests at authorization, settles responses at three days through ledger packets, and waits at least four days before executing a support-seeking operation. The President can stand down after an answer. `advanceUntilAttention` returns existing `consequence-return` attention when those answers first arrive, before execution, so Continue cannot skip the opportunity to stand down. Refusal/conditions do not silently confer backing. Confirmed partners only contribute through `coalitionBacking`; the same projection prices execution. Existing escrow restoration/charging and force reservations remain the single accounting path. No preview draws RNG.

Limited-strike objectives are carried through request, projection, issued terms and `InstrumentContext`. `strike-mission.ts` uses the existing readiness writer for military-capacity damage, adds real logistical-capacity damage for logistics, and opens bounded transport/infrastructure outage incidents for disruption objectives. Their service and output losses feed the existing `independentImpactPressure` path and recover on ten/fourteen-day clocks. Objective success and collateral harm remain distinct; collateral economy is parent-owned when a successful mission outage already owns the economic disruption. No second GDP writer or duplicate mortality event is added. Target-context labels, confidence/gaps, civilian exposure, service disruption, retaliation/ripples, conflict escalation and nuclear deterrence are all sourced from the shared projection; the primary target is not a school/hospital chooser. The visible unilateral authority reason now distinguishes the forty-eight-hour report from the separate sixty-day termination rule already implemented in `war-powers-report.ts`.

Limited strikes can request additional civilian assessment: three added days and a provisional risk reduction, with independent success/harm outcomes. `strike-assessment.ts` provides a pure deterministic modeled intelligence producer for ordinary player strike planning: a broad bounded fictional military/logistics/infrastructure/residential exposure context, rarer education/healthcare exposure, or no site case, from the operation seed. It derives source confidence from NGA capability and explicitly states uncertain use/occupancy. `AssessmentEffect.siteIssue` persists this evidence in `SecurityState.strikeSiteAssessments` at authorization; the request then freezes it through reload and execution. Scenario/operator producers can also provide validated `assessedSite` evidence; national statistics cannot substantiate school/healthcare identity or children. For an assessed service site, the independent harm draw also gates site damage and disruption; a miss does not force damage to a school/hospital. The shared `ImpactEvent` records physical harm once, keeps a stable issued-action parent ID, and accumulates service disruption/recovery separately. Report revisions preserve the optimistic revision check and charge only increases in cumulative losses. Foreign action incidents now publish periodic/final recovery assessments through the existing feed as domestic events do. The producer supplies fictional planning context to the shared incident model; it does not identify a real facility or replace the existing military success resolver.

The apparent weekly state lawsuit was an ambient governor-party heuristic, with no case/order/docket behind it. `state-suits.ts` now exports `StateOpposition`, `pickStateOpposition` and `stateOppositionThisWeek`; attention describes political opposition and links the state governor. Legal matters continue to require actual legal state. This selector correction changes no saved schema.


Player instrument actions use `issueAction` to reserve resources and persist a dated order. `resolveDueInstruments` rechecks live gates and affordability, settles the actual cost against escrow, resolves the operation once, and files the result. Continue stops at a new player operation report. The immediate resolver remains available to existing NPC/batch callers; that asymmetry is recorded rather than presented as unified scheduling.

Physical effects share the versioned incident model in [IMPACT_MODEL.md](IMPACT_MODEL.md). `impact-event.ts` defines input/state/report/effect contracts; `impact-catalog.ts` owns provisional coefficients; `impact-calculation.ts` owns daily pure calculations; `impact-lifecycle.ts` owns revision-safe writes and dated advancement. Condition and operator adapters reuse existing onset/response systems, and open their incidents on the week's closing day (`weekCloseDate`, `clock.ts`) rather than on the office day their phase runs, so an incident's daily clock is the same whether the week ran as one tick or as seven office days (DECISIONS.md §2.3, §2.4). New deaths are additive against live population; reporting cannot repeat them. Existing conditions retain macro costs, while independent incidents contribute output disruption through the existing macro-pressure reader. No second GDP or approval writer is introduced.

The Record's Impact assessments view and country dossiers render public report ranges and provenance, never the simulation's exact private toll. Saving retains the record; loading does not advance it. Day stepping and the weekly helper advance incident time explicitly. The model remains provisional pending empirical calibration and broader balance verification; it does not claim site-specific targeting or verified victim demographics without data.

## 4. The world dataset

`packages/world` is real, not a stub: zod schemas for `Nation`, `NationStats`, `NationTraits`, `NationBands`, `Relationship`, `Person`, `Organization`/`Cradle`/`Charter`, `City`, `State`, `SectorMixEntry`, and the whole-dataset `WorldDataset` shape (`nations`, `cities`, `states`, `districts`, `relationships`, `people`, `organizations`, `cradles`, `charters`), plus a dataset validator (`validate-dataset` CLI) checking shape, referential integrity, plausibility, and unit consistency. See `SCHEMA.md` for the full shape reference. The United States' playable slice (constitutional charter, starting cast, and named places taken from the City table; `usaPlaces` is a view over US cities), the seven-charter reusable catalogue, and the 196-government roster covering every `NationArchetype`, region and power tier (`nations.ts`) are authored in `packages/world/src/dataset/`. Per-nation trait ranges and candidate agendas (`traits.ts`) ship alongside them, with the seeded per-campaign roll living engine-side in `packages/engine/src/traits.ts` — data in `world`, logic in `engine`, per §2. The person pool ships as authored *draw shape* — name banks, role profiles, per-nation pool specs — with the cast itself generated per campaign in `packages/engine/src/people.ts`, including each person's bio, which `packages/engine/src/bio.ts` composes out of their seat and whichever of their five attributes rolled furthest from the middle rather than stamping from a template (see `SCHEMA.md` §5b). A person's **background is now authored draw shape too, and their attributes are derived from it rather than drawn beside it** (`dataset/schools.ts` — real institutions, at least one civilian one per nation, `schoolsFor` throwing rather than falling back; `dataset/background.ts` — the career, field and tier weight tables). `rollBackground` in `people.ts` schools and employs a person first, `backgroundPosition` squashes the summed weights into a position in [0, 1], and `atPosition` places them at that point *inside* their seat's authored window — so every role invariant that made an opposition leader disloyal and an intelligence director competent still holds exactly, while the variation between two candidates for the same chair is now earned by where they studied and what they have done since. This is what makes the dossier answer "why is this Secretary like this" instead of stating a number and captioning it (`SCHEMA.md` §5b). **The dossier's reading of a person is derived from that same arithmetic, not stored** (`packages/engine/src/person-assessment.ts`). `personAssessment` places every reading inside the window its own seat recruits from (`attributePositions`, the one yardstick, shared with the card's closing sentence), files up to two each way past a measured margin, and names the largest term in the background sum that pushed each one that way — `backgroundDrivers` returns the signed contributions of the school's tier, the field, the graduate degree and every career post, so the clause beside a reading is a fact the record holds rather than a cause asserted over it. Ambition is the one reading whose high side is filed against the president, because the standing rule already calls anybody past that mark a rival. **Thirteen seats are American and are generated only for the American pool** (`US_ONLY_ROLES`): the attorney general, the Hill's congressional leader and committee chair, and the ten department secretaries, alongside the US-only governors and bureau chiefs. No other charter seats any of them, and the career bank for an American seat is written in American institutional vocabulary. A portrait ref (`schemas/portrait.ts` + `dataset/portraits.ts`) is the facet tuple (heritage / gender presentation / age band / skin tone / attire / hair style / hair color / facial hair / eyewear / variant), and the dataset validator fails any ref that does not parse. See §4b for how those refs become faces. The organization registry (each body carrying authored `history` prose for bloc dossiers), the single homeland cradle (`CRADLE-POTOMAC`), and the geography graph are authored in `orgs-cradles.ts` and `geography.ts`, with their mechanics — distance-priced force projection, chokepoint-priced trade vulnerability, and cradle/conflict-gated org eligibility — in `packages/engine/src/geography.ts`. The cradle carries its own `geo: { lat, lon }`, equal to the US record's anchor; founding applies it to the player's nation alongside `region` and `neighbors` (`applyCradleToNation`, `packages/engine/src/founding.ts`), which is an identity for the United States; the founding contract keeps the step so saves and replay keep their shape. Derived targeting bands (`bands.ts`): archetype, power/wealth/military tier, alignment, and stability are computed from `stats` at assembly rather than authored, so a band can never disagree with the numbers beside it. **Conditions: three families, one mechanic.** `ConditionKind` (`packages/engine/src/condition-kinds.ts`) is now `ACUTE_CONDITIONS` — the six authored `geo.disasterExposure` kinds a country is struck by — plus `STANDING_CONDITIONS`, the seven it generates out of its own state (`unrest`, `recession`, `inflation-shock`, `unemployment-shock`, `fiscal-crisis`, `energy-shortage`, `housing-crunch`), plus `HOME_EVENT_CONDITIONS`, the five home events (`cyberattack`, `work-stoppage`, `bank-failure`, `mass-casualty-attack`, `wrongful-detention`), opened at home only by `condition-home-events.ts` from sourced base rates and eligible causes read off the campaign (a real-wage squeeze, a rate shock or slump, hostile captors, a week's intrusions into American systems), never Stafford-eligible, sharing the home queue's one opening a week (DECISIONS 6.5). Everything downstream is reused rather than duplicated: the same flag (`condition:<kind>:<nation>`), the same `conditionActive` scenario gate, the same `conditionSeverity` weight boost, the same director board pressure. **No save migration**: the family is a pure function of the kind (`conditionFamily`), never a stored field, so a condition written before this change answers correctly today. Onset differs by family — acute reads authored exposure, standing reads the nation's own statistics through `STANDING_PRESSURE` — and so does closure: an acute condition decays on a timer, a standing one is floored at `STANDING_FLOOR_SHARE` of its own pressure and cannot close while its cause is still true. A recession that timed out while growth was still negative would teach a player that governing is optional. **Congressional districts.** `District` (`schemas/district.ts`) is 436 authored rows (one per apportioned House seat plus DC's non-voting delegate), there because a player-facing map is a consumer a state average can't honestly serve: `delegations.ts`'s continuous seat-split over state lean is unchanged and reads no district, because the legislative mechanic never needed one. `dataset/district-figures.ts` carries the mass and political texture the same way `state-figures.ts` does for states — a redistricting-cycle refresh is a diff of one file. The validator (`checkDistricts`) enforces Law 1 twice: a state's district count equals its `houseSeats` (DC's one named exception, per `states.ts`'s own convention), and a state's district populations sum exactly to `State.population`.

**The rate lattice is the surface Law 8 now covers**. `macroDriftPhase` writes three rates today; the lattice extends it to eleven — `growth`, `inflation`, `unemployment`, `security.crimeIndex`, `demography.birthRate`, `demography.deathRate` and `economy.costOfLiving` at the nation, plus three deviations per state — under three named laws. **Law V1, one rate law:** `macro.ts` is the only module containing `nextValue`, and every rate gets its next value from that function and nowhere else. **Law V2, levels not laws:** a discrete named act (an instrument rung, a scenario reply, a casualty toll, a condition's weekly cost) may write a rate's *level* through `apply()` and may never write its evolution law — which is exactly why `conditions.ts` may set `economy.unemployment` and may not set `economy.growth`. **A standing program is not one of those acts.** An act happens once and a rate reverts from it; a program runs every week for as long as it is in force, and a weekly level write against a proportional reversion settles at the authored figure *divided by the reversion rate* — 25x on the economic rates, 100x on the political ones. `policy.ts` therefore splits its stances' deltas by whether `macro.ts` owns a law for the path: `RESTING_POINT_PATHS` (`policy-stats.ts`) — `economy.growth`, `economy.inflation`, `economy.unemployment`, `politics.legitimacy`, `politics.protestPressure` — leave through `policyRestingOffset` and shift the target `nextValue` reverts to, exactly as a condition's `macroPressure` does; the other eleven paths, which have no law behind them, still land as weekly levels through `governancePhase` and `policyPhase`, so a president's program stays visible in the phase that represents their choices. `governance.programRestingOffset` is the one reader for both halves of the world, because `macroDriftPhase` runs over every nation and must not have to know which of them has a cabinet. **Law V3, steering not billing:** a sub-national vital may select *where* a nationally-billed effect lands and may be read on a player surface, and may never add a second effect for a cause already billed at the nation. A state's vital deviation therefore chooses the address of a condition and never stamps a grievance of its own; `routeDomestic` already stamped the mark for the act that caused the deviation, and a second stamp would bill one act twice.

The schema half (§4e below), the business cycle, `vitalsPhase`, and the `crimeIndex` and `costOfLiving` laws are built. Birth and death rates have no law of their own yet.

**The business cycle** (`business-cycle.ts`) is the lattice's one intentional feedback loop, and it writes nothing: `cycleOffset(cycle)` is a pure `{growth, inflation, unemployment}` offset that `macroBaseline(runtime, cycle)` folds into the target `nextValue` already reverts toward, the same way a standing condition's `macroPressure` already does — so `macro.ts` stays the lattice's only producer of a next rate value, and a boom or bust cannot ratchet. `Campaign` gains `cycles: Record<NationId, CycleState>` (`{phase, enteredTurn, amplitude}`, one of `"expansion" | "peak" | "contraction" | "trough"`), written by a new `CycleEffect` that `macroDriftPhase` emits alongside its `nationStat` effect once per nation, per turn. `advanceCycle` evaluates the transition after that week's growth has settled: a minimum dwell per phase (`turnsFor`, fiction-time — asymmetric, expansions run 540 days and recessions 120–270), a hysteresis test against the baseline *without* the cycle's own offset (`NO_CYCLE`, so the machine never tests its own contribution — entry and exit thresholds differ by `0.6 × amplitude` so it cannot chatter across one boundary), and a per-week Bernoulli draw (`CYCLE_TRANSITION_RATE`, recompounded to whatever pace is in play). Every campaign opens at rest (`openingCycles`, expansion, zero amplitude) (a boom or bust already under way at founding belongs to the opening world, not to the cycle). `restingRuntime`'s "nothing is happening" comparator (used by `recession`'s onset, among others) keeps its exact pre-cycle meaning by reading through `NO_CYCLE` rather than a nation's live cycle, which leaves recession-onset sensitivity to the cycle as a separate, measured choice. No player surface reads the cycle yet; DESIGN.md plans it on `/nation/economy`.

**`vitalsPhase`** (`vitals.ts`) runs between `commitmentPhase` and `fiscalPhase` — after the week's obligations are known, before the country's own books read this week's people rather than last week's — and it is the lattice's one stock identity: `population' = population × (1 + (birthRate − deathRate + netMigration) × yearFraction)`, pure in `populationIdentity`. It owns `demography.population` alone; `economy.debt`, `gdp` and `gdpPerCapita` stay `fiscalPhase`'s. For the player's own nation, the delta is distributed across every state pro rata by that state's current population share (`distributedStateDeltas`, the last state in sorted id order absorbing the residue so the split always sums exactly), written as `StateEffect.deltas.population` (`StateRuntime` gains a live `population` field, opened at `base.population`), and the nation's own figure is then set to the sum of the states just written — never computed independently — so the two can never disagree, including under a floor-at-zero clamp. `sealFounding` already opens a state's population equal to its own states' sum (`applyStateMass`, `packages/world`, §4 above); a fixture that does not hold that invariant will see the mismatch resolve into the state total on its first turn, which is `founding.test.ts`'s own regression now. A nation with no state layer (everyone but the player) writes the identity directly. A state or district reading uses the live figures (`runtime.population`, and a district's live share of its state) rather than the authored snapshot. Birth and death rates have no law of their own yet; population moves off whatever rate currently stands, authored or eventually live.

**The coupling edges** extend `macro.ts` with two more rate laws, after a prerequisite they needed first. `ImpactProfile.unemployment` moved into `macroPressure` as a third field alongside `growth`/`inflation` (`condition-impacts.ts`), so a condition's unemployment cost shifts the target through `macroDriftPhase` the same way growth and inflation already do, rather than landing as the weekly level write `conditions.ts` no longer makes — a level write on a path `macroDriftPhase` reverts, settling at roughly 25x its authored figure, the same shape Law V2a already fixed for standing programs. The same pass found and fixed a second, independent defect: the Okun term (`Math.max(0, trend − growth) × 0.5`) was entering `nextValue` unnegated, so a country running below trend rested unemployment LOWER — backwards, and the same class of sign error as the other two channels, which `crimeBaseline` would otherwise have read. `security.crimeIndex` is now a lattice rate (`crimeBaseline` in `macro-baseline.ts`, `nextSecurity` in `macro.ts`): reversion toward a baseline reading unemployment above a 5% floor, cost of living above parity, authored corruption and border control, and wealth tier, with no crisis term of its own — crime has no direct condition pressure, so a condition still reaches crime only through the unemployment/cost channels it already has, never a second time. `economy.costOfLiving` is the lattice's one cumulative index: `costBaseline` computes the next value from the index's own previous value plus this turn's freshly settled inflation, reverting to it at a new `COST_REVERSION_RATE = 1` rather than the ordinary `REVERSION_RATE` — at 0.04 the gap between an always-slightly-ahead target and the index would close so slowly the index would barely move, the same "authored figure over the reversion rate" shape reached by a different route. Both new rates read their inputs from the runtime `macroEffectsFor` was handed — this turn's *starting* state, before `nextEconomy` moves anything — which is what gives the edge its lag rather than an instantaneous same-turn reaction. The city/treasury "money edge" remains open; the cost-of-living/crime-to-cohort-strain edge has landed since (below).

**The cohort-strain edge** (`vital-edges.ts`) strikes nationally. A geographic weighting by each state's live vital deviation from its anchor would need a state-deviation layer, which doesn't exist; vital edges strike nationally (`vital-edges.ts`), weighted by how exposed each cohort in the relevant axis is, never by geography. `vitalCohortEdges` compares this turn's settled cost of living and crime reading against where each started this same turn (`macroEffectsFor` hands it the pre-`nextEconomy` runtime alongside the just-computed values) and fires only on an upward crossing — `before < threshold ≤ after` — so a reading that merely holds above its edge, or falls back below one it already crossed, restamps nothing. Cost of living has two named edges (1.05 and 1.12 — a ratio against the value at term start, which is always exactly 1.000 by the index's own definition, so the absolute reading *is* the ratio), striking the "class" cohorts (`income-struggling` hardest, `income-affluent` least); crime has one national threshold (0.4, picked to sit above `crimeBaseline`'s ordinary resting range), striking the "place" cohorts (`urban` hardest, `rural` least). Every stamp is a plain `CohortEffect` through the existing `cohort` handler — no `DomesticSignature`, no `routeDomestic`, no `GrievanceMark` — which is the double-billing guard (DECISIONS.md §6.3) extended: a vital reaches a cohort through exactly one channel, and this is it.

**What holds a condition open is not what opened it.** A standing kind's onset reads an absolute figure, but its resolution now reads `standingHold` — the gap between the live reading and the same reading at that country's own resting state. The two were one function answering two questions, and the cost was measurable: 0 of 49 unrest spells closed in 300 turns, median open spell 253 weeks, because a severity floor of `pressure x 0.7` against a resolution threshold of 0.08 holds any reading at or above 0.114 open forever while the median country reads 0.35 on that axis with nothing happening to it. At a zero exit rate the equilibrium share is 1 whatever the onset rate is, so retuning onset would only have changed how fast the world saturated. Measured after: unrest fell from .74 of nation-weeks to .11, and the share of spells that close rose from .00 to .97.

**Law 8 — a gating stat has one owner, and no condition writes the stat it is gated on.** `macroDriftPhase` (`macro.ts`), placed immediately before `conditionPhase`, is the *only* weekly writer of `economy.growth`, `inflation` and `unemployment`; a standing program reaches them through the target rather than through a second weekly write of its own (Law V2 above). A condition declares `IMPACTS[kind].macroPressure` and that phase applies it; conditions no longer decrement growth directly. Without this, a `recession` whose onset reads growth and whose impact writes growth is a function of itself: its cause never eases, the recovery it closes on never arrives, and a campaign converges on growth pinned at the schema clamp under a permanent maximum-severity recession. The pairing is enforced by `law-eight.test.ts` against `STANDING_ONSET_READS` and `conditionImpactWrites`, not by review — and it immediately caught two live violations: shipped `unrest` raised the `protestPressure` its own onset reads, and a first draft of `housing-crunch` wrote the inflation it opens on. Both are fixed. The three rate kinds now go further than the law requires: a condition whose onset reads a rate may not carry that rate, and `recession`, `inflation-shock` and `unemployment-shock` carry no rate pressure and no approval drag at all (below).

Two further ratchets were found and closed by the same reasoning. **Crisis pressure moves where an economy rests, never what it currently is** (`macro.ts` `nextValue`): subtracting a pressure weekly makes the stat a running total of the crisis's duration, and since a nation-scoped condition's pressure is an order of magnitude above one proportional reversion step, the total wins and the stat walks to its clamp. Shifting the target bounds the system by construction — growth cannot fall below `baseline − crisis`, and recovery is produced by the model rather than allowed by a constant. Both terms in `IMPACTS[kind].macroPressure` are signed as *harm* rather than as a delta, so they enter that subtraction with opposite arithmetic signs: a positive `growth` term is a drag on output, a positive `inflation` term makes the country dearer, and `macroEffectsFor` negates the inflation channel on the way in. Authored as deltas, they inverted both channels — an energy shortage was disinflationary and a standing economic condition rested an economy *above* its own baseline growth, which left `recession`, whose onset reads growth, a kind that could barely open. **A standing condition shocks order on arrival and escalation only** (`conditionEffects`' `CostKind`): `regimeStability` had no restoring force anywhere in the engine — every writer pushed it down — so a perpetual weekly decrement reached zero whatever the president did. It now has one: `macro.ts`'s `nextPolitics` gives `regimeStability`, `legitimacy` and `protestPressure` the same mean-reverting law the economy already had, at a quarter of the economic rate, toward the authored value. That does not license a perpetual weekly decrement — a restoring force a president cannot outrun is still a ratchet — but it is why the world no longer converges on .47 order and .44 street from .65 and .30 authored. This is the *nation's* `regimeStability`, not the president's own `player.metrics.stability` — that metric has no restoring writer anywhere in the engine; every effect that touches it only decrements, which is a real gap `nextPolitics` does not close. Acute conditions may keep their weekly charge because they end. Continuous costs go only to channels that can recover: reserves (the budget refills them), unemployment (`macroDriftPhase` reverts it), and opinion (`opinionPhase` moves it both ways), and those are further scaled by `STANDING_PERSIST_SCALE` because every rate in `IMPACTS` was calibrated against conditions that last ten or twenty weeks rather than most of a term.

**A third ratchet, and it was the standing program.** Measured over 720,000 nation-weeks — 6 disjoint seed blocks of 8 seeds, 300 turns, 50 nations, identical seeds across configurations so every figure is a paired per-block comparison. A country under no condition but running an ordinary program rested **+.0487–.0500** above its own baseline growth, against a *maximum possible* `macroBaseline.growth` of .045; mean world growth ran **.0711–.0724** against a mean baseline of .0225. Nothing on earth could read as below its own trend, so `recession` — whose onset reads exactly that gap — held **.0004–.0007** of nation-weeks and was a kind in name only. The cause was Law V2 applied to a thing that is not an act: a program's weekly level write settles at the authored figure over the reversion rate. After the split described under Law V2a, on the same seeds: mean world growth **.0246–.0249**, the resting gap against the *authored* figure **+.0002–.0004** (from +.0466–.0479), the share of weeks far enough below trend to open a recession at all **.0479–.0555** (from .0064–.0066), and `recession` **.0028–.0048**. It is now the rarest standing kind rather than an absent one; making it common is the business cycle's job, not the program's. Removing the free ride also raised the kinds the program had been suppressing — `unemployment-shock` .027→.036, `inflation-shock` .030→.041, any condition .222→.257 — which is the 25x subsidy on a stance's unemployment and inflation deltas going away. **The rate law also rounds to six places rather than four:** the step is the gap times the reversion rate, so at four places a rate froze once it came within .0025 of its target (.01 for the political figures, which revert four times slower), which is larger than any resting-point shift a program can name. A quantum coarser than the law's own step stops the law before it arrives.

Nation records are authored as `NationInput` — `Nation` minus `bands` and `memberships` — and `dataset/index.ts` composes `applyBands(applyTrade(applyMemberships(applyGeography(applyStateMass(...)))))` to produce the assembled `Nation`. `applyStateMass` (`dataset/state-mass.ts`) runs innermost and writes the *playable* nation's `demography.population` and `economy.gdp` from the sums of `State.population` and `State.grp` — one level owns a fact, and every coarser level is a sum of it, so the states hold the country's mass and the country's figure is derived from them rather than authored a second time where it can disagree. It touches no other nation: every NPC is the finest authored level it has. `gdpPerCapita` is deliberately left authored rather than recomputed, so the existing unit-consistency check can still fail on a bad edit instead of being silently satisfied. The validator proves the derivation ran (`checkStateMass`: both sums exact, plus a nation-level metro-containment rule — `City.population` is a metropolitan figure and metros straddle state lines, so containment is only true at the nation). Giving the states a mass is also what let two long-standing proxies go: `cohortGeography` weighted an act by the *political displacement* it caused rather than by the people it reached, and `nationalCohortShares` weighted a state's mix by `electoralVotes` — which its own comment named as a population proxy, and which the floor of three makes a poor one. Both now weight by `population`, and both had to move in the same change: they are numerator and denominator of one ratio, so splitting them would bias every cohort strain figure toward large states. `applyTrade` (`dataset/trade.ts`) derives `stats.trade.topPartners` and `stats.trade.tradeDependence` from a gravity model over GDP, great-circle distance, land adjacency, shared region, and shared bloc membership; it must run after geography and memberships, which are its inputs, and before bands, because `bands.archetype` reads `tradeDependence` for its client-state and isolationist tests. Each nation keeps its partners above a share floor, capped by its fidelity (40 `full`, 25 `standard`, 12 `thin`); the playable nation is uncapped, because a partner missing from the President's list is a country the economic ladder cannot be aimed at, and every NPC keeps its lane to the President whatever its cap, because its moves against the President are the game. A country's form of government as a reader knows it is a separate baked table (`nation-government-types.generated.ts`, the Factbook's government type), read only by player-core's `governmentType()` for display; the charter remains what the simulation runs. `applyGeography` unites the authored neighbor lists with GeoNames' land borders (`nation-borders.generated.ts`) and makes every border run both ways, and gives a coastal country with no authored lane dependence its region's chokepoints, carrying the weights onto `geo.chokepointDependence` beside the ids in `geo.chokepoints`, so `chokepointExposure` reads how much each strait matters to that country rather than the strait's share of world tonnage. The gravity form is used rather than hand-authoring because 196 nations is 38,220 ordered pairs — authored pairs would be fabricated precision that drifts the moment a GDP moves — and the output is a plausible ranking and magnitude for the simulation, not customs data. Deriving it moved exactly one archetype: Belarus carries a real 0.26 dependence on Russia and is now `client-state` rather than `isolationist`, which is the client-state test doing what it was written to do. Two tests defend the scalability claims directly: an invented 51st nation is placed, validated, and shown to land in bands that already exist with zero code and zero content changes, and a registry (`stat-consumers.ts`) walks the zod schemas of **every surface a rule can read** — `NationStats`, `Nation.geo`, `NationTraits`, `Person`, the derived `NationBands`, `City`, `State`, and `SectorMixEntry` — to assert each leaf either names the declarations that read it, as `path#declaration` sites the test resolves against source, or is explicitly recorded as owing one. The owed sets are pinned per registry (`NationStats`: `medianAge` and `literacy`; elsewhere one band, `Person.affiliation` and the district cohort skews). Bands are in the walk too. There is no relationship band: how a nation stands toward the player is a fact about a pair, and `Nation.bands` is a static per-nation record built before any player exists. The set-piece rule that lets content name a nation deliberately, but never accidentally, lives in `packages/content/src/scenario-audit.ts` (`undeclared-set-piece`, `pointless-set-piece`). A second registry beside it (`dataset/units.ts`) answers what each of the same 53 leaves is *measured in* — a `UnitSpec` per leaf, walked by the same schema-driven test, so a field added without a unit fails the build. Its `formatFigure()` is the one place a scale word is chosen, which is what stops two screens disagreeing about whether an authored `2_100_000` is two million or $2.1 trillion (DECISIONS.md §4.3; `SCHEMA.md` §3). It is also the one place an `index` becomes a graded word rather than a decimal — `gradeOf`, magnitude rather than quality so it reads true on the fields where a large number is bad news; see `SCHEMA.md` §3.

**Two layers per record.** A record's figures and its authored layer are separate. The figures for every roster government the authored roster does not state in full are baked from pinned, sourced seed tables by `scripts/bake-nations.mjs` into `dataset/nation-figures.generated.ts` (`NATION_FIGURES`, with per-row provenance: sourced, prior, judged, clamped — `SCHEMA.md` §9.1), and `composeNation(draft, figures)` (`dataset/compose-nation.ts`) lays a `NationDraft` over them, the authored value winning wherever one is stated, before `worldNations` is assembled. Composition happens where `worldNations` is defined rather than in `dataset/index.ts`'s assembly chain because `traits.ts`, `people.ts`, `schools.ts` and `bake-cities.mjs` read `worldNations` directly, and a country composed later would be missing from all four. The bake is a separate, deliberate step that reads the built world package; it refuses to write if any row fails to parse, if composing any authored record changes it, or if the built dataset fails validation, and it prints the drift between authored figures and their sources without moving any.

### 4b. Imagery: how a person's record becomes a face

Every face in the game is drawn at render time from the person's own record. There is no image catalogue: `Person.portraitRef` carries either the facet tuple the person system drew (every generated person) or a `face:` reference holding a whole authored genome (the President), and `faceForPerson` (`packages/world/src/dataset/faces.ts`) turns either into a `FaceGenome` (`packages/world/src/schemas/face.ts`).

**The genome.** Continuous structure genes (head shape among eight archetypes, width, jaw, chin, length, cheekbones, forehead, build; eye size, spacing, tilt, openness; nose and mouth proportions), discrete parts (24 hair cuts in four textures, 10 facial-hair styles, 8 frames, 12 head coverings, 15 garments, jewelry, makeup) and palette choices (six tones with shade and undertone, eight hair colors, seven iris colors, a 34-cloth palette). Every registry is append-only, because an authored face is stored as positions in them. `face-code.ts` encodes a genome as a fixed-width, versioned base-36 string of about ninety characters; a later version appends slots and never reorders them.

**Generation honors the record.** `generateFace(facets, seed, context)` renders every facet exactly (presentation, tone, facial-hair family, eyewear, attire, hair style, and hair color at the age the facet was drawn, solving the graying onset so the face shows that color then and keeps aging after) and draws everything the facets leave open from `face-priors.ts`, seeded by the person id, so one person has one face on every screen and in every session. Heritage shifts a feature mean by at most a sixth of its range and never gates a part. The context carries the nation (dress conventions: thobe and ghutra in the Gulf, kaftan and kufi in West Africa, the Nehru jacket, the peci, the kippah; the American flag pin), the name tradition read back out of the person's name (`nameTraditionOf`, `name-traditions.ts`: a Sikh-named man in a dastar, a covered Hindu-named woman in a dupatta rather than a hijab), the person's seats (the bench sits in a robe), and their traits, of which only public ones move the resting expression: a portrait never shows corruption, loyalty, ambition or integrity.

**Rendering.** `packages/ui-web/src/portrait/` (the `@admin/ui-web/portrait` subpath, `renderFace`) draws a genome as layered SVG in a 200-unit frame: layout from the genome and the person's current age (`layout.ts`, including the superellipse jawline that runs from a pointed chin to a square jaw as one exponent rises), then backdrop, hair behind, garment, neck, ears, skin, marks, beard, mouth, mustache, nose, eyes, brows, hair in front, head covering, glasses, jewelry. Shading uses SVG filters and gradients (a softened terminator, ambient occlusion, a cool rim light, iris and lip gradients, a cloth weave, grain and vignette), which the browser runs once when it rasterizes the image. Age is a render input: graying (temples first, a dyed flag holding color), recession, lines, lid hooding, jowls, neck bands and thinning move with the years while the genes stay fixed. Mood (`neutral`, `warm`, `stern`, `concerned`, `strained`) and treatment (`official`, `wire`, `archival`, `silhouette`, `redacted`) are render inputs too, never stored.

**The resolver.** The player resolves a person's genome, reads their current age off the campaign clock (`currentAge`, `packages/player-core/src/dossier/person-age.ts`: founding age plus years since the oath, the same number the dossier prints), renders, and memoizes the result. A reference that is neither form (the unfaced President) resolves to nothing and the portrait draws a monogram. The map-first resolver is not built yet.

**The President's face is built at founding.** The face builder's controls are data (`packages/ui-web/src/portrait/face-controls.ts`); the founding person step draws a face once a presentation is stated, edits every gene, and stores the genome as a `face:` reference. `composeLeader` passes it through untouched and `identityRefusal` checks its presentation against the draft's. The founding screens are rebuilt in W3 of the map-first program.

The facet model in `portraits.ts` is what the person system draws from; the studio console keeps a read-only `portrait-batch` descriptor so job files already on a machine still list. Scene stills keep the generation pipeline (`scene-batch`).

### 4c. State character: an authored economic signature, routed

`State` (`packages/world/src/schemas/state.ts`) carries a required `character: StateCharacter` — a closed six-axis economic signature (`industrial`, `agricultural`, `energy`, `tech`, `services`, `defense`, each 0..1, summing to 1) plus `urbanization` and `federalDependence`. It answers which of the fifty states a nationally-scoped decision actually lands on, and how hard. All 51 states (50 states plus DC) are authored against plausible real-world economic composition (BEA/BLS-shaped figures). `SECTOR_AXIS` (`packages/world/src/dataset/sector-axis.ts`) is the one place the closed 23 `SectorId`s fold into the six axes; it is an exhaustive `Record<SectorId, StateEconomyAxis>`, so a new sector fails the build until it is assigned an axis.

`packages/engine/src/domestic-signature.ts` is the runtime consumer. A `DomesticSignature` (axes and/or sectors, optional urban/federal tilts, an optional `values` ideology pull, a magnitude, a slug and a reason) folds sectors through `SECTOR_AXIS`, scores each state with `stateAffinity`, scales by magnitude and lean elasticity, clamps to `STATE_ROUTE_CAP` (0.025) under `STATE_ROUTE_TURN_CAP` (0.04), signs the delta toward the administration (by `caucusAnchor`, scaled by reach, via `towardAdministration`), and emits a `StateEffect` per state whose `|delta| ≥ 0.002`. Every emitted effect also carries an `addMark` with the signature slug and reason. `routeDomestic` returns effects, not packets — callers splice them into their own packet the same way `resolveImpact` and `gazetteerEffects` do, so `verify-engine-reachability` correctly treats it as a compiler rather than a player action. Iteration is over `STATE_IDS` in declaration order so the effect list is byte-identical run to run.

**The ideology wire.** `values?: Partial<IdeologyVector>` on `DomesticSignature` is what lets a reply about something other than an industry — an identity or rights question, a religious-liberty dispute — still land with real state and cohort texture. A reply's `pull` (the same `IdeologyVector`, declared on every position via `AlignmentPullShape`) moves `player.alignments`, a single national accumulator; `values` is what gives an act with no economic axes state and cohort effects. Nothing extra is authored for it: `COHORT_MOTIVATION[cohort].beliefs` (`demos.ts`) already carries a differentiated `IdeologyVector` per cohort (rural sits 0.15–0.25 toward `authLibertarian`, urban 0.1–0.2 the other way), and `cohortAffinity` (`route-cohorts.ts`) now folds `signature.values` against it directly, term for term with `exposure`. A state carries no full `IdeologyVector` — only `lean` — so `stateAffinity` reads it through `stateValues`: `economicLeftRight` and `authLibertarian` both read `lean` directly (a Republican-leaning state reads positive on both), `nationalistGlobalist` reads urbanization inverted. `stateValues` is a stated proxy: authoring a real per-state `IdeologyVector` is an open question, and `stateAffinity` is the only site that would change if one is authored. `IDEOLOGY_AXES` (`world/schemas/person.ts`) is the fixed iteration order both folds share, mirroring `STATE_ECONOMY_AXES`'s role for the economic term.

Content authors the same shape as `DomesticSignatureShape` (`packages/content/src/domestic-signature.ts`) on both `position` and `instrument` commitments. `scenario-gazetteer.ts`'s `domesticSignatureEffects` compiles it through `routeDomestic`; `scenario-cast.ts` strips the authored field and puts the StateEffects on `commitment.resolved` beside gazetteer City/State/Sector effects. Instrument commitments carry optional `resolved` so the same splice lands on the instrument packet after `resolveAction`. The first non-scenario producers are the executive-order and legislation catalogues: optional `domestic` on `ExecutiveOrder` / `LegislativeAct`, spliced through `routeDomestic` (and `beliefSplitEffects`) from `signOrder` and every `enactmentEffects` path.

`packages/engine/src/party-beliefs.ts` is the party-platform consumer of the same signature. `signatureStance` maps folded axes plus urban/federal tilts into the three ideology axes; `partyReading` is the signed dot product of a ticket's `beliefs` with that stance; `beliefSplit` is how far apart the two majors sit (0..1); `selectPartyReading` returns both majors framed as administration/opposition for the decision memo; `beliefSplitEffects` emits `OpinionEffect.polarize` of `BELIEF_SPLIT_POLARIZATION × beliefSplit` (0.008 peak — under the joint `MAX_TURN_POLARIZE` ceiling, never a separate allowance). `figureTarget` optionally takes the figure's caucus `PartyBeliefs` and the act's signature and adds `BELIEF_WEIGHT × partyReading`. There is deliberately no `partyBelief` scenario condition.

`STATE_CONSUMERS` records the eight character leaves as read by the domestic signature (`domestic-signature.ts#stateAffinity`, `#stateValues`), the policy portfolio's state industry shares and the state dossier, and the three `character.vitalAnchor` leaves as read by regional opinion's economic term (`regional-opinion.ts#economicShift`), the state vitals reading and the state economy board. Three leaves and not one entry for the object: the registry walk unwraps a nested object, so a single `vitalAnchor` line would leave three fields unaccounted for. The dataset validator's `state-economy-sum` check still enforces the partition — all six axes of every state's economy sum to 1 ± 0.002 — and `state-vital-anchor-centers` holds the population-weighted mean of each anchor column to 0 ± 0.005, which is what stops 153 hand-authored deviations from re-baselining the country they are deviations from.

**State and district dossiers.** A dossier is a pure function of `Campaign` and an id that returns a plain object a screen renders with no logic of its own. player-core has the district dossier (`world/district-dossier.ts`: the district's authored figures read against its parent state's *live* lean and delegation disposition) and the state readings (`world/state-vitals.ts`, and `world/state-approval.ts`: the state's approval from the engine's `stateApprovals`, the call this week's projected vote makes there, one plain divergence line where the two point opposite ways, and the weekly `stateApprovalHistory` with a note that it starts at the oath). The state dossier DESIGN.md plans at `/nation/state/:id` carries the six `StateEconomy` axes, the seated governor's party and disposition (`GovernorStanding`), the delegation's disposition and both Senate seats (`StateDelegation`, apportioned from the chamber's composition rather than modeled per member), pending claims owed an answer before `expiresTurn` (soonest first; an expired claim writes a lasting adverse `GrievanceMark`), recent marks aged in weeks against the live turn, the state's cities and its cohort mix. Its composition and the map-first screens are not built yet; a district opens as `?district=` on the state route.

### 4d. The cohort layer: twenty-nine ids, ten axes, and a state's population mix

`packages/world/src/schemas/cohort.ts` adds the demos layer's vocabulary: `COHORT_AXES` (ten: `age`, `class`, `schooling`, `place`, `work`, and since 2026-10-11 `race`, `sex`, `nativity`, `disability`, `veteran`), `COHORT_IDS` (sixteen cells across them), and `COHORT_AXIS`, an exhaustive `Record<CohortId, CohortAxisId>` on the same precedent as `SECTOR_AXIS` above — a seventeenth cohort id fails the build until this map says which axis it cuts. `cohortsOfAxis(axis)` is derived by filtering `COHORT_AXIS`, never a second hand-kept list, so the axis-to-cells mapping cannot drift from the map that owns it.

`StateCharacter` (§4c) gains a fifth field, `cohorts: StateCohortMix`, additive beside `economy`, `urbanization` and `federalDependence` rather than a restructuring — sixteen shares across the same five axes, each axis a closed `.strict()` object built off `cohortsOfAxis` so the schema cannot disagree with the vocabulary it is built from. This is a state's authored population mix: grounded in real demographic patterns (age, class, schooling, place, work), from which every national cohort figure is meant to derive at runtime, the same relationship `economy` has to the domestic-signature router.

`cohorts` is **required**: all 51 states (50 states + DC) carry complete five-axis demographic cross-cuts. The schema enforces that every state holds the full mix; there is no national cohort table to disagree with state-specific data. All 255 axis sums (51 states × 5 axes) are validated at parse time by `checkStateCohorts` to sum to 1 ± 0.002, enforced on the same pattern `checkStateEconomies` uses.

**Motivations.** `packages/engine/src/demos.ts` holds `CohortMotivation`, the twenty-nine-entry `COHORT_MOTIVATION` table, Law 3 constants (`LENS_TILT_FLOOR`/`CAP`, `COHORT_MIN_SPREAD`, `LENS_CENTERING_TOLERANCE`), and `cohortLens()` — derived as `normalize(OPINION_WEIGHTS ⊙ tilt)`, never authored. The `cohort-lens-centered` unit test holds the share-weighted mean lens within 0.02 of `OPINION_WEIGHTS` on every driver for each axis.

**Strain state.** The same module holds `CohortStrain` (`{ createdTurn, cause, weight, valence, decayRate, reason }` — Grudge / GrievanceMark shape), `STRAIN_DECAY` (asymmetric by valence: −1 at 0.06, +1 at 0.10), `OMISSION_DECAY` (0.03), `MAX_COHORT_STRAINS` (6), `openingCohorts()`, `CohortEffect`, and `applyCohort` — the sole writer of `Campaign.cohorts`. Eviction drops lowest weight, then lowest `createdTurn`, then `cause` ascending; survivors keep append order. Founding and the campaign fixture seed empty lists. **Save v26** opens the same empty lists on a v25 save that lacks them (`campaign.cohorts ?? openingCohorts()`); empty strain is inert, so the election on the load turn matches the pre-layer count.

**The read path.** `selectCohorts` returns each cell's standing, `cohortReading` is the sentence, and `weakestCohort` is read off **displacement, not support** — support carries the national mood identically for every cell and says nothing about who *this administration* hurt, which is the question a cohort layer exists to answer. `crosstabs` (`packages/player-core/src/polling/crosstabs.ts`) composes all sixteen rows grouped by the five axes, each with its share, its support and its own sentence. Turnout is its own reading beside it (`turnout.ts#selectCohortTurnout`), and the vote reads it through one driver.

**Derived readings.** `nationalCohortShares` (EV-weighted state mix; age axis drifts by `growthRate` × years; never stored), `netStrain`, `cohortWeights` (salience ⊙ tilt), `centeredStrain`, `cohortCompositions` (each cohort's five-bloc mixture: the founding shares tilted until the cohort's neutral-record vote equals its CES partisan baseline, `cohort-blocs.ts`), `cohortStartingPoints` (the mixture's blocs, each read through `blocLogit` at the valence of the record seen through the cohort's own lens), `cohortSupport` with `STRAIN_SCALE`. Boundary law 2 identity: the share-weighted strain term is zero on every axis, so strain moves a cohort against the others and not the aggregate. The baseline is no longer calibrated away: it fixes the composition, and a cohort's starting point is what its blocs read, so the partisan gap between groups falls out of the blocs. The headline never reads a cohort, and a cohort's starting points agree with it across an axis within a stated tolerance, not exactly: one tilt parameter per cohort cannot make every axis re-aggregate and every baseline hold (DESIGN-VERIFY measured baseline drift of up to 5.5 points when raking forced re-aggregation), so the tilt matches each baseline and re-aggregation is left approximate (DECISIONS 5.8, 5.9).
