That weighted sum, `raw`, becomes a valence (`VALENCE_SCALE` 12 × (`raw` − `VALENCE_MIDPOINT` 0.52), plus the honeymoon) which five partisan blocs read at their own slopes (the President's identifiers and leaners, independents who lean neither, the opposition's leaners and identifiers); the headline is the share-weighted sum of their approvals (`approvalFor` in `opinion.ts`, `electorate.ts` for the formula; `SCHEMA.md`, "The electorate"). There is no compression toward an anchor, no damping of deltas by polarization and no second step on the headline: polarization acts once, inside the blocs, by setting how far apart the parties start and how little the identifiers move. Order at home, an army that can answer, a treasury that can pay and a country other capitals take seriously are reasons people think well of a government, and they reach the score through the public at the public's speed (`OPINION_DRIFT`) rather than as arithmetic billed the week the fact changed. There is no composite headline.

Removal pressure is five terms, none of them a composite: `PRESSURE_WEIGHTS = { unpopularity: 0.36, alignment: 0.2, decisionHistory: 0.14, congress: 0.14, floors: 0.16 }`, with `unpopularity` reading `1 - approval` directly, the headline once. The `congress` term and `decisionHistory`'s reading of falls only are as they were; the design reads the headline's net moves over ten weeks and deletes the `congress` term, which double-counts party control (`DESIGN.md` §4.8, slice 4, not built). A solvent, orderly, well-armed government is not safe from removal if the country loathes it. See `BEAT_THRESHOLDS` in `removal.ts`.

**The electorate is five blocs, and the cohorts are mixtures of them.** `electorate.ts` holds the blocs (`ownId`, `ownLean`, `pure`, `oppLean`, `oppId`), their shares (`Campaign.electorateShares`, drawn at founding inside a symmetric range around Gallup's 2025 shares), the valence, the bloc formula, the ballot and the caucus reading; `polarization.ts` holds the stock; `cohort-blocs.ts` holds each cohort's composition and the turnout displacement; `force-issue.ts` holds the one issue term built (hawk and dove, Pew 2019). Every surface that says who a decision angered reads the cohort layer (`selectCohorts`, `weakestCohort`, the reason sentences on their strains) or the three blocs a player is shown (`displayRollup`: the President's party with its leaners, independents, the other party with its leaners; `OpinionSample.blocs` keeps them week by week) rather than inventing its own numbers. The three coalitions (working households, business and industry, institutions and capital) are retired: `selectCoalitions`, `coalitionApproval`, `COALITION_LENS` and the coverage driver are deleted with `coalitions.ts`, because three constituencies read through lenses were a second opinion of one public, and the bloc formula is the one place that opinion is computed. Nothing is written back onto `player.metrics.approval` by a bloc, a cohort or a display roll-up: the headline is the one computation (`selectElectorate`), settled by `recomputeApproval`. Readers outside the engine still named the retired figures when this was written (2026-10-13): player-core's `economy/coalitions.ts`, `polling/crosstabs.ts`, `electorate/electorate.ts`, `world/war-powers-reading.ts` and the glossary, and `apps/web`'s `nation/electorate/approval-build.ts`; the surfaces slice moves them (`DESIGN.md` §5, §8 slice 8).

### The summons: fourteen institutional clocks

**`runningClocks`** (`packages/player-core/src/summons/running-index.ts`) lists every clock the presidency carries, the ones with a subject this week first with their due dates, then the quiet ones. DESIGN.md places running clocks in Brief (`/brief/clock/:id`).

`summons/types.ts` draws the line an attention item cannot: an `AttentionMatter` is a standing condition — a war is on, a chair is empty — and a summons is a *dated obligation* the constitution puts on the calendar, which runs out whether or not the President looks at it. A summons is composed, never authored: every figure on the brief (`SummonsBrief` — title, clock line, figure, history, posture, counsel, door) is read from campaign state through an exported engine selector, and where the record is empty the brief says so rather than inventing a first cycle that never ran. `compose.ts` holds the shared reading — `urgencyForWeeks`/`urgencyForDays`, `stanceForUrgency`, `weeksPhrase` — so every clock ranks and phrases the same way.

`clocks.ts` concatenates two files into one `SummonsSpec[]`, split by which branch starts the clock rather than by how it is drawn: `clocks-legislative.ts` holds the obligations Congress creates (appropriations, the shutdown, a bill on the desk, war-powers reports and terminations, a treaty awaiting ratification) and `clocks-executive.ts` holds the ones the presidency creates for itself (a rulemaking's effective turn, the CRA disapproval window, a nomination's stage, a ruling due, acting-authority expiry, an election milestone, a removal beat). Both halves produce the same `SummonsSpec`, so `registry.ts` above them never learns the difference. `dueSummonses` filters to the clocks that currently have a subject — a spec whose `due(campaign)` returns nothing contributes nothing at all rather than an empty row — and ranks lapsed before closing before standing.

**`SummonsClass` decides whether a clock stops the week or waits.** `interruption` is reserved for a clock whose lapse is constitutionally irreversible — the money stops, the force must be withdrawn, the seat is contested, the administration ends — and stops the week; `document` waits with the rest of the open work. An interruption is presented one at a time, and dismissing it loses nothing: it is the same document the open-work list holds. The map-first presentation is not built yet.

**Badge ownership holds by construction.** Attention rows and the summons layer's clocks both come out of `attentionItems`, which collapses two readings of one `subject` into one row — a bill on the desk, an unauthorized force, an order under review, a removal beat — so there is no second field to keep in sync. The appropriation clock stands down once the government has actually closed (`inShutdown`): the money running out and the doors closing are two readings of one fact, and past the grace window the shutdown clock is the truer of the two and carries the same door to the budget. The CRA disapproval window (`clocks-executive.ts`) yields to the bill once a resolution of disapproval is actually introduced — at that point it is a bill, tracked there — and its own expiry unanswered is deliberately never painted as adverse to the President: a window closing on Congress's power to undo a rule is the administration's good news, not a countdown against it.

**Pending work counts down on one reading.** `desk/pending.ts`'s `selectPending(campaign)` is every attention row that names the day it is due, read before `collapseBySubject` (`attentionRows`) so two waits on one subject both survive, with the constitutional calendar left out (an election is a date, not a wait). Each `PendingItem` carries what it is, who it waits on (`AttentionItem.waitingOn`), every page it belongs on (`AttentionItem.about`: `nation:CN`, `person:…`, `treaty:…`, `sector:…`), its door, game days left from `daysBetween(campaign.calendar, dueDate)`, and progress from `AttentionItem.opened` to the due date, or from a statutory clock's own window. `pendingAbout(campaign, key)` is a page's slice. The trade producers (`desk/attention-trade.ts`) raise investigations under way, findings made in the last week, partners' retaliation due, and Section 122 orders running out. Every row stays ambient: the answer a wait is for stops the run once through the engine (`advance.ts` `answersReturned`, below), so a row never holds Continue on news the President cannot answer. The app draws every wait with one part, `Countdown` in `packages/ui/src/world/Clock.tsx` (a ring of the wait gone, days left in its middle), on the subject's own page (a country's "Where things stand", the act page's "Already under way", the trade view for goods-wide investigations), the Brief's "Waiting on others", and the calendar (`calendar/sources-pending.ts`, kind `pending`, statutory clocks left to their own source).

**Attention reaches the part of the screen it is about.** The navigation carries a badge on the place each owed row opens (`waitingCounts` in `apps/player/src/shell/nav.ts`; the phone bar sums a group's places). A held Continue shows "N waiting on you" as a control that opens the first matter holding it, and on a phone replaces the held button. A `Section` takes `waiting` and marks its heading with the count. Every `Page` lands on its address's hash once the target exists and rings it for a moment (`layout/landing.web.ts`), so a row, a stop line on the Brief (each now opens its card through `pendingHref`) or a countdown opens on the exact card.

**The Record re-read path.** A document that lands in week eleven is gone from the queue by week twelve, and the only reason it was worth reading is that a presidency can be asked about it later. `allReadings` lists every clock the presidency carries, running ones first, and `summonsReading(campaign, id)` answers any id at any time — a `SummonsReading` whose `due` is absent is not a stopped clock erased, it is the spec's own quiet account ("no bill is on the desk") composed fresh rather than a closed door. A reading composes against live state rather than a reading carried in from the queue, so a clock answered in the meantime reads as answered rather than replaying a stale countdown. The Record's whole claim is that the document outlives the moment.

## 7b. The balance registry

`packages/content/src/balance-registry.ts` catalogs every tunable constant
that decides how a campaign feels, at the layer a rebalance actually happens
on. A rebalance done at the wrong layer, or blind to the other layers, can
fix one defect and still make `balance.test.ts` net worse, because the
catalogue-layer numbers it feeds (`POLICY_STANCES`' per-turn magnitudes) and
the mechanic-layer ceilings were tuned against the behavior it changed. The
registry lets all the layers be seen at once.

**Four layers, coarsest first**, each with a live example in the registry:
`global` (`OPINION_WEIGHTS`, `PRESSURE_WEIGHTS` — reweights the whole score),
`mechanic` (`REVERSION_RATE`, `STANDING_PERSIST_SCALE`, `CLASS_COOLDOWN` — one
system's own constant), `catalogue` (a single `POLICY_STANCES` row, a single
`IMPACTS` row — one entry inside an authored table), `derivation`
(`TREASURY_REFERENCE` — not a constant but the formula that turns a raw figure
into a score). The registry's own doc comment states the five-step
methodology: name the symptom in harness terms, read every entry whose
`governs` plausibly touches it, change the narrowest layer that could produce
it, re-run `runBalanceHarness` and read the WHOLE report rather than only the
assertion that motivated the change, and commit the constant with its
reasoning.

Every `value` in the registry is the live import, never a retyped literal —
the same Law 1 discipline authored data follows, so the registry
cannot state a number the source disagrees with. The paired test
(`balance-registry.test.ts`) checks the registry's metadata (every entry has
a real `governs` reason, a real layer, no duplicate id, at least one entry per
layer) rather than walking a schema the way `stat-consumers.ts` does, because
these are scattered exported constants rather than leaves of one zod tree —
there is no single shape to walk. Coverage is not yet exhaustive (roughly
thirty of the engine's and content's tunable constants are catalogued); like
`stat-consumers.ts`'s own history, it grows by the same discipline that built
it rather than in one pass.

**What this is not.** A live constant-override / rebalance CLI — a tool that
takes `{ constantId: multiplier }` and reruns the harness with those values
injected — is the natural next tool this registry enables, and it is not
built. Every constant catalogued here is still a frozen `export const`;
rebalancing today means editing the source, rebuilding, and re-running the
harness by hand, which is the workflow this document's own methodology
section describes. Named as the next step, not attempted in this pass.

## 7c. The benchmark layer

`packages/engine/src/benchmarks.ts` answers the one question a 0..1 reading
cannot answer for itself: *compared to what?* `metricBenchmarks(campaign,
metricId)` returns reference marks on the metric's own 0..1 scale — the same
scale the dial reads, treasury included, which is why the module reads
`treasuryHealth` rather than restating the division. Pure, deterministic,
sorted ascending, clamped into `[0,1]`. It is engine-local authored data, not
a world dataset field: nothing here describes a nation, everything here is
either a published real-world figure, one of this engine's own thresholds, or
a reading off the campaign's stored series. No schema change, no save change,
no `stat-consumers.ts` entry.

**The honesty rule is the design, and `kind` carries it.**

| kind | what it may claim | who gets one |
|---|---|---|
| `historical` | a published, verifiable figure that lands on the metric's scale by arithmetic alone | approval (Gallup landmarks) and treasury (real federal outlays, converted through the USD-millions money unit) |
| `gate` | a line this engine itself reads | stability, standing, readiness, treasury — never approval, which the regime floors deliberately exclude |
| `own-record` | where this campaign has already been | every metric, once a term exists |

Stability, standing and military readiness get no `historical` mark and are
not supposed to. There is no published scalar for how stable the United States
was in a given year on a 0..1 scale, and a fabricated mapping would teach a
player something false while looking authoritative. `benchmarks.test.ts`
asserts that absence per metric, so a later hand cannot quietly add one.

The gate marks are read from the live constants (`REGIME_METRIC_FLOORS`,
`REMOVAL_METRIC_FLOOR`), which were named and exported for this — the same
Law 1 discipline the balance registry keeps. A benchmark cannot state a
threshold the rule disagrees with.

**Surface.** No player surface draws the marks yet. A range track should name them in its legend and in its spoken reading, skip the own-record kinds it draws itself, convert treasury back into money, and drop any mark outside the term's span rather than clamping it to an end: a landmark the presidency never reached must not be drawn as one it is standing on.

## 7d. The studio console

**Running it.** `pnpm studio` at the repo root is the only command. It runs
`apps/studio/launch.mjs`, which refuses to start, naming the exact thing that
is wrong, when a workspace package the server imports is unbuilt or when port
4400 is taken; those were the two failures that previously presented as a
blank page or an import stack. (Any IDE launch config lives under `.claude/`,
which is machine-local and untracked — the package script is the portable
entry point.)

**No sign-in gate.** The console is a single-operator, localhost-only tool
with no auth: every request that reaches port 4400 is treated as the one
operator who can reach it. There is no session, no login/logout route, and
nothing to attribute a write to beyond "whoever is running this".

**The frame.** Every page renders into one application shell (`view.mjs`, with
its stylesheet in `theme.mjs`): a persistent left rail grouping the routes by
what they manage, a command bar carrying the breadcrumb and a global search
field reachable with `/`, and the workbench. A horizontal nav could not survive
the route count, and — more to the point — could not carry a search field that
reaches across artifact kinds, which is what a five-figure corpus needs before
anything else.

One server, four halves. The **command** half is the front door: `/` is the
command center and `/explore` the scenario explorer (both below). The
**dataset** half (`apps/studio/dataset-routes.mjs`) is the management surface
over the world the game is built from, rendered from the metric registry
(`docs/SCHEMA.md` §3, "The metric registry") rather than by walking records —
so a field the registry does not describe cannot appear as a bare key, and
every figure carries its type, unit, bounds, provenance and reader:

| Route | What it does |
|---|---|
| `/nations` | The index: every record with region, archetype, the three tiers, stability, population and GDP formatted through the unit registry, posture, and how many overlay edits are pending. Filters by name, region and archetype; sorts by the columns a roster is scanned by. No inline write: posture is edited like every other authored field. |
| `/nations/<code>` | The profile: the authored record surface by surface (record, identity, geography, doctrine, traits, statistics by group, order of battle, bands), one registry row per field — label, definition, the figure as a player reads it with the raw value beside it, a track between the schema's ends with the words for what each end means, a type chip, a provenance badge (authored / derived / runtime) and who reads it. Traits show the authored midpoint, the campaign band and this seed's roll side by side. The order of battle prints catalog labels with ids and people-per-unit. **No cast.** People are generated per campaign and never stored on a country; the page says so and links to the seed's roll under Cast. |
| `/nations/<code>/edit` | The typed editor: every authored scalar the record carries (exactly the set `scalarLeafPaths` and the registry's `editable` agree on), each input typed from the schema — a number field carries the schema's own `min`/`max`/`step`, an enum is a closed select, a boolean a two-option select — with the definition, bounds and scale words beside it and any pending value shown against source. Posts to the overlay; a value outside bounds or options is refused at save through `metricRejection`. |
| `/metrics` | The metric catalog: the registry itself, filterable by surface, provenance, value type, free text, and "owed a reader". Read-only, because the code is the record. |
| `/forces` | The force-asset catalog (every id with echelon, domain, what one counts as and people each) and every authored order of battle read against it, with the validator's own verdict per nation. |
| `/bands`, `/regions`, `/geography`, `/orgs` | The derived views (`world-views.mjs`): band distributions and placements, regions as memberships, chokepoints and projection costs, compacts and cradles. |

The **cast** half (`/people`, `/portraits`, `/roles`, `/names`, `/backstories`)
browses a seed's generated roll and edits the role profiles that produce it.
The **edits** half is the dataset overlay (below). The **content** half drives
the generation loop:

| Route | What it does |
|---|---|
| `/` | The command center: the pipeline as a funnel (in a run → drafted → owed a decision → published here → live), each step a link to where that stage is worked; an alert list of only the conditions that have an owner and a next action; the run queue ranked by what is still owed; and the thinnest classes, which is where the next run should be aimed. It computes nothing itself — every figure is read from the module that already owns it, so it can be stale but never disagree. |
| `/explore` | The scenario explorer: every scenario in the checkout, whatever stage it is at, as one filterable table. Facets over class, cast, stage, gate, fires-once and condition kind, each counting against what the *other* facets already left; free text over id and subject; sorts including draw weight, gate depth, answer space and impact. Backed by `scenario-index.mjs`, which streams the shards once and holds a row per scenario against their mtimes — the corpus's own selector index carries no prose, so it cannot answer "which one is the ferry scenario" or "what does answering it cost". |
| `/search` | The global field's landing page, answering across scenarios, runs, nations and people at once — an operator who remembers a word does not yet know which kind of artifact it belongs to. |
| `/scenarios` | Run dashboard: every job as a card with its pipeline stage (generating / review / shipped), a done-versus-failed progress bar, and totals across all runs — items open, items failed, decisions awaiting a human, scenarios shipped, spend. |
| `/scenarios/new` | The run composer: a grid over the real class and cast vocabularies, seeded with the classes the corpus currently has least of, totalling live as it is filled, read back into the `class:cast:count[:steer]` cells the CLI takes. Creates the job and materializes its briefs in one post, driver fixed to `session`. The billed OpenRouter path stays in the terminal, where its typed spend confirmation has an honest equivalent. |
| `/scenarios/<job>` | Run detail: per-item status with the journal's own failure reason and timestamp, the review actions (materialize, scaffold, decide, finish), readiness defects, and every journaled scenario as a summary card. |
| `/scenarios/<job>` (image kinds) | `portrait-batch` and `scene-batch` are registered in `job-kinds.mjs` as non-reviewable: they get items, progress, failure reasons and spend, and explicitly no review pass, because `studio-cli` models review for the two prose kinds only and a console that scaffolded a record no CLI command could finish would be worse than one that says so. Before they were registered, eleven of fifteen runs on a working machine rendered as a stack trace. |
| `/scenarios/<job>/status.json` | The progress snapshot the detail page polls every four seconds while items are open, so a run in flight repaints without a reload and stops polling when nothing is left open. |
| `/scenarios/<job>/scenario/<id>`, `/library/<id>` | The scenario reader (below), for a job output and a shipped pack scenario respectively. |
| `/library` | Every shipped pack scenario read live from `@admin/content` — class, cast, beat and reply counts, word count, weight, conditions. |
| `/articles` | Article cover desk: real `composeArticle` pieces from the engine, not dummy copy. |
| `/articles/<id>` | CoverPackage form. Three brief fields with Content's helper text sit next to the plate upload; recipe is a closed enum; the plate must cite a versioned lock in `content/cover-locks/`. Kicker and headline render under a full-bleed plate. Save draft is always allowed. **Publish fails closed** until `publishCoverPackage` accepts — no brief, no cover. Local run: `pnpm studio` then open `http://localhost:4400/articles/press-border-shipment`. |
| `/articles/<id>/plate` | The uploaded plate for the cover preview. |
| `/cover-locks/v1/scan` | The house lock scan once Design drops `content/cover-locks/v1/scan.jpg`. 404 until then, which is why a plate citing v1 cannot publish yet. |

**The reader and simulator.** A scenario renders as a document, not a JSON
dump: meta as chips, conditions and locus in prose, `particulars` as the wire
strip the game draws, and the opening set in a serif at reading size. Under it
sits a client-side simulator — beats reveal one at a time, replies present as
choices, and picking one shows its full commitment, remembered line, press
headline, and flags. Counterpart, leader, and title are live controls, and the
placeholder interpolation mirrors `scenario-cast.ts`'s `renderText` exactly,
including leaving an unresolved token standing (styled, so a token no charter
fills looks deliberate rather than broken). A decision-tree view beside it lays
the branches flat for scanning what an answer costs. Nothing here re-implements
the gate: review state still moves only through `scaffoldReview` /
`finishReview` / `recordDefects` from `studio-cli`, so a click here and a
`studio scenarios review` run cannot disagree.

**Everything lists in pages.** The console assumes a corpus in the five figures, so no view renders an unbounded list: runs, run items, review queues, the corpus, and the cast all page server-side with filters over the whole set rather than a sample. The dashboard's per-job summary is cached against the mtimes of the files it was computed from, so an unchanged job costs a stat call instead of a re-read of its journal, review record, and ledger share.

### The dataset overlay (`/overlay`)

Dataset editing is a diff against source, never a second copy of the dataset.
`packages/world` stays the seed and stays pure — the game reads TypeScript and
never reaches for a database — so the overlay is a local JSON file
(`apps/studio/.local/overlay-edits.json`, git-ignored), merged over the seed
for display and review, and reaches `packages/world` only through an explicit
publish.

**Shape.** Each row holds one changed field: `entityKind`, `recordId`, a
dotted `fieldPath`, `baseValue`, `newValue`, and a published stamp. Saving an
edit replaces any pending edit to the same field, so what publish applies is
never a sequence whose order decides the result.

**Privilege.** The console has no sign-in gate, so there is exactly one role —
`admin` — held by whoever can reach the port. `canPublish` remains a function
of a role for shape-compatibility with the overlay pipeline, but every operator
resolves to `admin`.

Publishing rewrites the file the game is built from, which is the only action
in the console with a consequence outside it.

**What is editable.** Scalar leaves only, derived from the record rather than
listed, so a new `NationStats` field is editable without a code change — and
the metric registry's `editable` flag names the same set, which the paired
test holds to `scalarLeafPaths`. Lists, weighted mixes, `bands`, `memberships`,
and `id` are all excluded, each for its own reason: the first two have to agree
with something outside the record, the next two are derived at assembly and
would be overwritten at the next build, and `id` is a rename across the whole
dataset. A proposed value is judged twice before it is written: by the
overlay's own shape rule (a number stays a number) and by `metricRejection`
(inside the schema's bounds, one of an enum's options, on an authored field),
so a save is refused with the schema's own words rather than a publish failing
later.

**Publishing** (`apps/studio/overlay-publish.mjs`) plans before it writes.
Staleness is judged against the text of `nations.ts` on disk rather than against
the imported dataset: a publish rebuilds `@admin/world` but cannot replace the
copy already resident in the publisher's own process, so a second publish in one
session judged against the import is judged against pre-publish values.
An edit whose `base_value` no longer matches source is refused, not applied —
the dataset is still hand-edited by people who are not using the console, and
publishing over one of their changes would discard it silently. A nation's
edits are validated as a group through `NationInput`, since two changes can
each be in range and be nonsense together. Writes go through
`apps/studio/nation-source.mjs`, which resolves each path segment against the
*immediate* keys of the object the previous segment opened — `production`
appears under both `energy` and `military`, and `netProduction` must not match
`production` — and refuses any field authored as a constant reference rather
than a literal. Source is written and rebuilt before any row is stamped: a
failed write must never leave rows marked applied that source does not carry.
A build failure reverts the file. The US playable slice is authored in `dataset/usa.ts`
alongside its charters and hand-written cast, not in the nation table this
publisher writes, so its profile offers no editor at all.

### The cast is generated, so the profiles are what a console can edit

`/people` browses every person the seed generates — all of them, filterable by nation and role and searchable by name, with a detail view per person. What it does not offer is per-person editing, because a person is not stored: the pool is a pure function of the seed, the fifteen `roleProfiles`, and the per-nation `personPoolSpecs` (`packages/world/src/dataset/people.ts`), and the same seed regenerates it exactly. How deep each nation's bench runs is read off the record's `fidelity` (8 for the playable nation, 3 for a `full` or `standard` country, 1 for a `thin` one), which is what keeps a 196-country roster from carrying three unmet people per seat for every microstate in every save. An "edit this person" form would be editing something the next roll discards.

`/roles` is the honest version of that intent: the role profiles themselves, with their attribute bands, age range, and ideology spread editable. A save rewrites the profile's own object literal in `people.ts`, rebuilds `@admin/world`, and reverts the file if the build fails — the same discipline as the nation-posture edit, and now the same code: `apps/studio/source-patch.mjs` isolates one object literal by brace counting while ignoring braces and quotes inside strings *and comments*. That last part is load-bearing on these files specifically, since their provenance comments contain apostrophes, and a scanner that treats one as a string delimiter swallows the rest of the file.

`/portraits` reads facet coverage off the generated cast rather than a lookup table, and each facet value drills through to the people carrying it.

## 8. CI

`.github/workflows/rebuild-ci.yml` runs on every branch push: install, build, typecheck, lint, then every `scripts/verify-*.mjs` by glob in one step (it runs all of them, reports every failure, and refuses a glob that matched nothing), then the unit suites in three tiers and the Playwright e2e run, across every package in the pnpm workspace. The directory is the list of gates, so a gate runs the day it lands; `scripts/README.md` documents the convention. It is the repo's only build pipeline. The balance harness has its own weekly matrix workflow (`.github/workflows/balance-harness.yml`, `BALANCE_PARTY`). A schedule fires only from the default branch, and as of 2026-10-08 main does not carry that file and every workflow is disabled on GitHub, so the gates run only when someone runs them locally.

A gate named `verify-X-proof.mjs` proves `verify-X.mjs` can fail: it builds a fixture with the violation the rule exists for and requires the gate to reject it. A rule that is only ever run against clean code proves nothing about whether it still works.

**Ratchets.** Some gates cannot pass today's tree without forgiving what is already wrong with it, and hold that debt in `scripts/baselines/<gate>.json` through one shared reader (`scripts/baselines/ratchet.mjs`): a finding the baseline does not forgive fails (NEW), a forgiven finding that is gone fails until the baseline comes down (STALE), and a baseline raised above the one at HEAD fails (RAISED). `--update-baseline` rewrites a baseline and refuses to raise. They are `verify-css-architecture`, `verify-disclosure`, `verify-foreign-truth`, `verify-icon-registry`, `verify-opinion-causes` and `verify-unused-primitives`. `verify-willingness-route` reads `willingness-pending.json` and `hazard-draws.json` from the same folder. `verify-engine-reachability` reads `scripts/capability-ledger.json`, where each row is due by a named wave and is judged by call site (DECISIONS.md §12.5); genuinely internal readings go in `scripts/engine-internal-readings.json` with a reason.

### Contextual legislative agenda (2026-10-10)

The bill document and Congress comparison now share `billFundingLines` and
`billRestingLines` in `player-core/legislation/bill-reading.ts`: annual
`LAW_OFFSETS`, their duration and the separate enactment charge are read once.

The engine's existing registry, eligibility and bill lifecycle remain the only
legislative path. `legislative-acts-domestic.ts` adds eighteen hypothetical
packages: coverage, rural care, equal protection, voting access, policing,
sentencing/reentry, housing, apprenticeships, paid leave, small-business credit,
grid investment, permitting, data privacy, public integrity, disaster recovery,
unemployment support, veterans' transition and border adjudication.

Availability reads the existing economy, public pressure, term progression,
active natural-disaster declarations and US war participation (including joined
wars). Calendar progression opens standing programs; crises open responses.
Existing pending/enacted/recurrence checks prevent duplicate bills and renewals
before expiry. `transmit` still reserves one administration slot, costs Hill
standing, enters referral, and grants no enacted benefits. Normal committee,
markup, floor, signature, lapse and override logic applies. Rights measures use
the ordinary legislative track, not an invented reconciliation shortcut.

`LAW_OFFSETS` owns continuing spending, receipts, transfers and investment;
macro resting offsets feed the existing economy. Enactment feeds the existing
stat, alignment, domestic-state and cohort mechanisms. No ethnicity-based
preference is inferred. These are aggregate political/economic consequences;
health outcomes, discriminatory acts, housing completions, individual rights
litigation and implementation backlogs are not separately simulated. Numbers
are design assumptions awaiting dedicated balance calibration, not empirical
forecasts. Natural-disaster recovery excludes unrest and pandemic declarations.

Player-core builds the proposal preview from that same act and fiscal registry.
Congress's `LegislativeAgenda` filters only eligible proposals by subject,
paginates four at a time, and opens a focused baseline comparison. It never
renders eligibility functions, turn gates or numerical unlock requirements.
Funding, competing interests, current counts and state/household consequences
are shown before transmission. `HillAct` and the existing commit/receipt/Record
path handle authorization. Returning from the receipt recomputes the agenda.

### Intelligence collection and disclosure (2026-10-12)

The existing tasking phase, covert surveillance rung and domestic surveillance
act issue `CollectionProduct` records through `applyIntelligence`. Collection
and exposure use independent seeded streams. Reports snapshot the supported
facts and name gaps rather than deriving new allegations from a country or
state average. `collectionAgainst` filters useful current products for analytic
support; archived reports remain readable after collection stops. The Security
screen reads the archive through `collectionReports` and uses its existing
comparison, authorization and receipt flow for `shareCollectionReport`.

The external-intent pool can include an assessed border incident involving the
tasked government. `collection-report.ts` ignores unresolved incidents and
assessments older than one collection cycle; a filed finding freezes the
incident ID, parties, assessment turn, conclusion and evidence confidence in
`CollectionFinding.basis`. Its wording preserves uncertainty about who fired
first. An `undetermined` review remains undetermined even if the filing's
source confidence is high: source confidence describes the report-level
collection capability, not the certainty of each conclusion or a calibrated
probability. Sharing selects that exact dated finding. The public assessment
already adjusts the incident's relationship pressure where justified, so
disclosure adds no second diplomatic modifier or inferred blame.

Sharing snapshots only the selected findings and their original uncertainty for each recipient; later disclosures append new receipts and exact retries add nothing. A shared-report decision binds the recipient snapshot, never the source archive. Withheld findings and gaps stay outside that snapshot; it grants no global access or automatic trust bonus. A
narrow economic-support decision can read a fresh issued covert warning about
the recipient, while preserving compact and competing-tie requirements. Broad shared organization membership no longer satisfies
`hasIntelOnNation`; the existing capability gate remains. Normal country readers no longer reveal counts of hidden ties. Selected report findings are individually disclosed; broader access auditing remains open. DHS public-state OSINT is separately scoped to existing public conditions and
requests. Both blanket resident-surveillance actions refuse without charging;
private domestic collection authority and process remain unfinished.

**Comparison against the published record (`player-core/economy/comparison.ts`, `apps/web/src/shell/compare/SeriesChart.tsx`, `apps/web/src/nation/economy/Compare.tsx`).** `comparisonView(campaign, rate, windowId)` is a pure read: it lines `campaign.economyHistory` (weekly samples, 104 weeks kept) up against `referenceWindow` from the world's metrics module, by months since each series began, never by calendar date. The campaign's weekly fraction is brought to the reference cadence by the rule `checkComparison` declares (the last reading in each month, or each three-month block for growth, times 100); a block the campaign has not finished is not drawn. A preset window the pack does not cover for that rate is listed as unavailable with the refusal reason, and selecting it draws nothing rather than falling back. Nothing here writes to the campaign, so choosing a rate or window cannot move state, time or randomness. `SeriesChart` draws two or more series on one axis with a gap where a period has no figure, distinguishes them by weight and dash as well as color, and carries a table of every figure.

**Countries beside each other (`player-core/economy/peer-comparison.ts`, `apps/web/src/nation/economy/ComparePeers.tsx`).** `peerComparisonView(rate, peers, windowId)` reads the World Bank pack for two to four countries over calendar years. A preset window is available only if every chosen country is covered for that measure; otherwise it is listed as unavailable with the first refusal reason and selecting it draws nothing. It never includes the campaign's simulated countries, and the panel says so; a view of simulated countries would be a separate mode with its own definitions. The Economy panel's mode switch picks between this and the presidency-against-United-States view. `SeriesChart` takes a `variant` (0 to 3) that varies color and dash together so up to four countries stay distinguishable without color alone, and chooses round-number axis ticks.

**History retention (`economy-archive.ts`).** The weekly tick keeps the last two years of the economy at weekly resolution (`ECONOMY_HISTORY_WINDOW`, 104) and folds each week that leaves the ring into a monthly archive (`Campaign.economyArchive`), so a presidency of any length keeps its whole course at the resolution the comparison charts read, at about twelve samples a year. The archive keeps the last reading of each month rather than a mean, so it holds only values the economy printed. `economyRecord` joins the two for the economy history and comparison selectors. An older save has no archive and gains none: the weeks it never kept are not invented. The same ring and fold (`recordWeek`) keep two observed records the week closes (`closeWeek`, after the select phase and the polarization ceiling, stamped with the economy sample's turn): `opinionRecord` (`opinion-record.ts`: the six judgments, polarization, every cohort cell's support and the approval of the three blocs a player is shown, `own`, `independent` and `opposition`, a month keeping its last week) and `relationsRecord` (`relations-record.ts`: the United States' trust in each government, its trust in the United States and the tension, the first entry whole and every later week only the governments that moved at a thousandth, a folded month keeping all its weeks' changes). Both are observations of derived readings (DECISIONS §1.10), recorded once at a fixed point in the week (§1.9), read by nothing that decides an outcome and drawing no random number; a test plays twelve live weeks with the records kept and thrown away and finds every other field identical. The read side is player-core's `polling/record-trend.ts` (`TrendPoint`, `changeOver`, `trendOf` with four- and thirteen-week changes, `recordStart` with the "record starts at load" line), `polling/opinion-trends.ts` (`opinionTrends`, `crosstabTrends`, carried on `ElectorateView.trends`) and `world/standing.ts` (`Standing.trend`: ours, theirs behind the services, tension), which the country file carries on `NationFile.standing`.

**The demographic cohorts (DECISIONS 5.8, 2026-10-11).** The five added axes reach households only through material channels in `COHORT_MOTIVATION`: sector `exposure`, `programReliance`, `attention` and published-style `baseTurnout`. Their `beliefs` are all zero and their `spread` is 0.45, and tilts are neutral except disability and veteran status, where the program itself is the channel (services for disability, security and services for veterans), written so each axis cancels within the lens-centering tolerance. So an act that is about federal programs or a sector reaches these cohorts through `cohortAffinity`'s existing federal and exposure terms, and none of them reads an identity as a belief. Routing: `DomesticSignature.channels` (`cohort-channels.ts`) names the policy channels an act moves, each from -1 to 1: `civilRights`, `immigration`, `healthAccess`, `disabilityPrograms` and `veterans`. `CHANNEL_REACH` says how directly each channel bears on each of the 29 cohorts (0 to 1, each row's basis written beside it), and `cohortAffinity` adds `channelAffinity` to its exposure, federal, place and values terms, so the same routing that writes cohort memory also carries a channel. Channels reach cohorts only: `routeDomestic` never reads them, so a channels-only signature moves no state's lean (DECISIONS 5.10), and the strain they write is cohort memory that cannot move approval (5.9). The catalogue declares them on ten acts (health coverage, rural care, equal protection, voting access, police accountability, sentencing and reentry, paid leave, veterans, border adjudication, immigration reform) and two orders (border controls, entry suspension, both restrictions, so negative); a test requires every channel to be moved by something in the catalogue and every identity cohort to be reached by a channel with reach of at least 0.3. `player-core/policy/reach.ts` reads `routeCohorts` to say, on the act and order desks, which groups an act helps and costs (`reachByGroup`, `reachSentence`). Known gap: there is no dedicated disability act, so that cohort is reached through health coverage, paid leave and the veterans act.



### Recorded court procedure and substantive bill text (2026-10-12)

`judicialReviewPhase` advances typed proceedings through threshold, interim relief, lower merits, discretionary review and final disposition. `decideCase` uses each participating justice's own legal threshold and one shared case draw. A six-justice quorum is required; equal division preserves the recorded lower judgment. An adverse lower order judgment pauses prospective authority for both operative and unimplemented orders. A hard Desk case offers acceptance, further review, or further review with a separate stay application; silence accepts after seven days. Review alone does not suspend the judgment. The court decides the stay independently, and only a grant can restore otherwise lawful prospective authority. Acceptance or default applies the lower remedy once. Completed historical cases are immutable; legacy pending challenges enter this process prospectively. The statutory-priority family binds an enacted defense authorization and an actual production order, including party-limited relief and possible Supreme Court construction of the authorization. Agency-procedure, federal/state grant, election-access, consular, housing, wage and interception matters have implemented subject paths; broader process and lower-bench integration remain open.

Markup for housing supply, health coverage and clean-grid bills changes actual provisions. `proposeBillText` offers the authored narrower alternative; `bill-stages.ts` preserves each chamber's text and sends disagreement to conference. The President may recommend a passed text, but both chambers must agree before presentment. `actOf` selects enacted substance and `lawOffsetForBill` selects its annual outlays and investment; proposal comparisons and bill documents use the same authored alternatives. Other bill families retain their prior markup abstraction.


`scenarioOccurrenceKey` keeps the authored template distinct from a staged repeat. The existing case/docket/attention and reply pipeline now carries that occurrence through URLs, frozen snapshots and once-only silence charges; once-only and family templates preserve their current rules.

Order and rule landing records retain realized non-debt contributions. `orderRemedy` removes the known contribution at most once through the existing effect gate. Legacy missing measurements remain unknown. For attributable relative movements, `order-lineage.ts` replays the recorded sequence with the affected owner removed or reduced, retaining unrelated interventions and saturation. An absolute set or unexplained drift invalidates attribution; the case and order readers then show unquantified relief rather than assert an exact restoration.


### Individual Congress, judicial vacancies and report-bound decisions (2026-10-12)

`officeSuccessionPhase` (`succession.ts`), after `judicialVacancyPhase`, draws deaths for every occupied modeled office from `officeholder-mortality.ts` (the 2024 CDC/NCHS life table at `OFFICEHOLDER_MORTALITY_RATIO` 0.35, one keyed stream per person and year, the day drawn so deaths do not cluster; never the President or a judge; the heads of the bodies below the cabinet and the Vice President are drawn, White House staff are not) and fills each office by its own path: `vacancy-law.ts` dates House specials (125 days, or a federal general election at least 57 days out) and decides whether one changes party (`houseFlipChance`: the holding party's margin is its edge in the seat's lean, `seatLeans`, plus the national mood, approval less 0.5 for the President's party and against the other, flipping at `1 / (1 + exp(margin / 0.15))`, one keyed draw per vacancy, calibrated to 19 of 132 specials since 2001; an independent's seat never flips) and applies each state's Senate law (NCSL, 2026-07-29), `withMember` keeps the roster, the chamber count and the delegation in step through a change of party, and `reseatPosts` reseats posts and panels with the election writers. Governors pass by the state's line of succession (`governorSuccessor`: a ticket state keeps the governor's party; a separately elected lieutenant governor or a legislative leader is of the party `specialElectionParty` reads off the state's lean), Secretaries to an acting deputy through `appointments.ts#vacates` with the `died` departure, an agency head to the agency's first assistant as acting under the Vacancies Act (a board runs on its quorum, an office with no acting waits for the President), the Vice President to an empty office (`vicePresidentDied`: the office, the Senate's presiding officer and the line's first place empty together, and the President's nominee takes office on confirmation by both houses, `nomination-stages.ts#voteResolution` with `OfficeDef.confirmedByBothHouses`, `vice-president.ts#withVicePresident`), foreign leaders to `foreign-politics.ts`'s `deathInOffice`, which falls back to a stand-in in government where the cast drew no second candidate. A count is taken over the seated members (`congress.ts#seatedOf`): an empty seat is neither a yea nor a nay, and majorities, the two-thirds bars and cloture shrink with it. A measure that takes an authority from the President (`bill-stages.ts#restrainsThePresident`, now including a resolution terminating an emergency) is counted in the `against` frame whoever sponsors it.

`congress-members.ts` seats persistent people against the existing district/delegation composition. Elections retain eligible incumbents and replace seat occupants through the existing person generator and effect gate. `member-votes.ts` computes each member's decision from party, individual ideology, represented state and personal rapport before `voteOn` sums the count. Legacy aggregate fallback remains for records without a roster. Bill passage separates Senate cloture from majority final passage; `recordFloorVotes` stamps a completed vote and the UI refuses to expose an unstamped individual forecast as a roll call. `whip-count.ts` derives delegation tallies from member rows when available. The Congress Members tab searches all recorded seats and links to persistent dossiers; bill files expose stored individual calls. Broader procedural types and belief-based forecast calibration remain open.

`judicialVacancyPhase` performs one namespaced, saved annual assessment per occupied Supreme or circuit seat after the founding year, including anonymous circuit incumbents. Age comes from founding age and elapsed calendar years; empirical total-population mortality with documented judge adjustments and an explicitly calibrated pension-eligible retirement model determine ordinary vacancies. A retirement marks eligibility; a death marks an identified person dead; both vacate the institution. The existing nomination and confirmation pipeline seats successors and writes actual simulated service provenance. Completed opinions retain their recorded voters. Research and distribution evidence live with `COURTS-RESEARCH.md`; general early resignation is not supplied by the pension-retirement model. Case-specific recusal excludes a justice whose recorded government legal review concerned the challenged order, independent of nomination chronology.

The content binder can distinguish Chief Justice from associate vacancies using the existing nine Supreme Court seat institutions. An adverse-lower order-case cast instead binds to the engine's open court-response obligation and freezes its order, legal question, rule state and seven-day due date. The authored reply's typed `orderReview` command calls the existing court resolver; it closes that obligation atomically with the conversation reply. The Desk hides the parallel court family row while the authored case is open, so it is one decision on screen; `authoredCourtResponseIds` keeps that hidden engine case live during family seating. Deferral mirrors one return date before the original deadline. Continue, explicit acceptance and hand-off use the shared court resolver; the authored case uses the engine response date and carries no separate authored silence charge.

`scenario-report.ts` matches fresh issued collection findings and freezes the source with the cast. The scenario case snapshot carries the report/share binding, and expiry withdraws an obsolete decision without charging silence. `scenario-reply-memory.ts` keeps classified report responses in the intelligence feed; the content audit rejects a public press line on such a case. Authored requests must not be inferred from a receipt: a `CollectionShare` proves a recipient received a report, not that it asked for military or economic help. The typed report gate also supports no-result products with gaps. The reviewed no-result decision requires an actual matching open tasking before offering closure; there is no runtime prose generation.


### Authority, outreach and localized harm (2026-10-12)

Executive order preparation selects a typed subject before checking authority. `external-emergency.ts` matches IEEPA findings to actual public foreign pursuits; `order-subjects.ts` binds relief to a state request and construction redirection to a named enacted project and armed-forces declaration. `military-construction.ts` owns appropriation records and subsequent obligations. The existing order signature and effect writer perform the redirection together, preserving prior obligations and avoiding a second Treasury charge.

Member outreach runs through the existing issued-act calendar and lobbying ledger. An eligible broker follows weighted standing caucus, state-delegation and leadership connections together with directed recorded cooperation on the requested issue (`person-network-influence.ts`; same party and chamber 0.02, shared state 0.5, caucus leadership 0.75, recorded issue contact 1, all planning values). The strongest connection per recipient wins; weight then person id orders traversal. Office and weighted connectivity bound an effort at eight downstream recipients. Traversal stops at two hops, visits a recipient once, attenuates accepted pressure by depth (`0.06 / depth`) and stops a route on refusal. Every recipient applies independent policy and constituency preferences. No committee membership roster is inferred from chair, ranking and balance. Direct and brokered requests share the daily attention budget. Standing connections are derived from the current seated records, never stored as contacts. Private lobbying deducts 0.2 × capped decayed personal grudge weight (`ties.ts#personalGrudgeWeight`) inside the disposition term, which preview and resolution both consume once; public appeals still read voters' standing at home. Calls and envoys approach a shared surplus of 0.06 above each directed `relationshipAnchor` (`engagement.ts#ROUTINE_TRUST_SURPLUS`), including durable treaty shifts; other favorable departures consume the same room, and swapping kinds or initiator does not reset it. The person graph labels biography overlap as inference and excludes hidden traits before ranking, caps or geometry.

Incident population accounting now debits the nation and its states together. Known condition geography receives casualties first; only overflow or unlocated casualties are distributed through the existing state allocation utility. Cohort and domestic political reactions remain separate from physical population accounting. The incident revision gate prevents duplicate casualties; same-turn order identity prevents a refreshed authorization from charging its political and fiscal effects twice.

The existing glossary supplies contextual Congress, court and collection explanations through `ExplainerNote`. Modeling comparisons show current recorded opinion causes separately from projected alternatives. These readers are pure and do not draw simulation randomness.


### Integration contracts: remedies, budget instructions and executable content (2026-10-12)

`applyNationStat` remains the authoritative live statistic writer. `recordStatLineage` records intended relative moves on paths with owned order/rule contributions. `ownerWeight` computes limited/final relief by replaying that history and then writing through the same effect function. Repeated relief is idempotent; a late temporary-stay restoration cannot reactivate a final remedy. Missing or invalidated history remains an explicit unknown, and no replay refunds debt or sunk costs. The agency-procedure and federal/state grant matters reuse the proceeding clock and actual action records. Agency notice/comment/site-review facts constrain both lower and individual Supreme reasoning; recorded prior legal participation constrains the eligible bench.

`reconciliation.ts` requires an actual concurrent budget resolution adopted by House and Senate before granting the eligible pending bill its reconciliation route. The instruction is bound to fiscal year and exact bill occurrence. The Bill screen requests the vote and displays its recorded result; later bill passage and presidential action remain separate. Mixed nonbudget provisions cannot obtain the route merely from a catalogue label.

Authored legislative transmission uses `conversation-report-actions.ts` and the existing legislation resolver. The content gate declares the eligible administration act; the final commitment checks availability again. A scenario cannot promise enactment from introduction. Other typed report actions likewise recheck collection, closure, relief or state-response authority at commitment.

`populationToll` routes authored material casualty bands through live demographic accounting when applied, including delayed replies. It shares the existing nation/state allocator with condition losses, so national and state population remain consistent without resetting intervening demographic change. It does not equate exposure, attribution and political support.


`breakSenateTie` adds the presiding officer's ballot separately rather than flipping a senator's vote. New vote totals can therefore contain one more ballot than seated senators. The roll-call reader names that casting voter and direction; historical legacy totals are not recalculated.


### Private cooperation and week-boundary consistency (2026-10-12)

`cabinet-reach.ts` distinguishes an official's access from ambition. Current cabinet service or recorded counsel supplies access; outgoing accepted contacts each make their own willingness decision, and distinct willing recipients bound spread. Those private responses remain in the existing causal record's optional `internalDecisions`; player readers exclude them. Public consequences still use the same effect pipeline and primary causal stamp.

`closeWeek` recomputes approval on the fully assembled next-turn state. Honeymoon and scandal contributions read the turn, so recomputing before incrementing it left the stored aggregate stale until another action. This aligns the actual daily clock and forecast settlement without widening a forecast band.

Trade and inspector-general authored decisions freeze real runtime records into the existing scenario snapshot. `scenario-trade.ts` checks active retaliation and the actual response resolver; inspector-general gates pin an open matter. `conversation-report-actions.ts` performs commitment-time validation and delegates to the existing tariff/directive action. An authored answer cannot manufacture a prosecution, a foreign retaliation, or an enacted appropriation.

`election-access-court.ts` is the first independent state-election subject in the general court record/read path. The audit and prospective remedy use state effects; the election calculation reads exposed participation and stores actual state calls at certification. Completed cases and election calls stay historical after personnel or equipment changes. This slice remains an integration draft pending corrected state-response timing, appeal metadata and implementation uncertainty.


Private decision visibility is carried by the frozen conversation through its causal provenance. The Record uses foreground-ledger access so the President retains their own internal inspector-general response without making it public; internal replies produce no feed item and classified replies use the intelligence feed. Public press is rejected for these private casts.


Covert mission success and public attribution are separate outcomes. `covert-exposure.ts` preserves the surveillance stream and gives higher covert rungs their own saved-seed stream at the existing counterintelligence-based exposure rate. Packet provenance records the realized visibility; diplomatic ripples, authored aftermath visibility and NPC feed use that finding. Institutional openness alone no longer makes every hidden operation public. The coefficient remains a provisional game abstraction, not an empirically calibrated detection rate.

The President's own covert deed flags remain a record of ordered work, not evidence that its target identified the actor. `deedPressure` excludes covert flags, including legacy flags with no exposure evidence: attributed success already changes the target's directed trust and tension through `exposedCovertTargetEffect`, and visible failure uses the existing grievance path. This prevents both undiscovered-operation knowledge and a second exposure charge in NPC disposition. `deedContribution` does not write received/from flags for an unattributed foreign act, so authored callbacks cannot name a perpetrator the player has not learned. Previously saved received flags are preserved; their historical attribution is not reconstructed by this change.


Construction cash uses the existing appropriation phase and law-offset reader. `militaryConstructionPhase` commits active source authority, pays prior source and redirected obligations, and records `sourceSpent`, `lastCashOutlay` and `lastWorkTurn` through the existing project effect. A stay, cancellation or adverse judgment stops only the applicable new commitments; existing liabilities remain payable. `lawShift` prices current recorded cash rather than the canceled source’s entire authorization and does not apply generic investment smoothing to already-paid cash. `lawShiftOverTenYears` uses remaining payable/authorized balances over ten years rather than extrapolating one week forever. No additional debt or Treasury mutation path is introduced.


### Source-backed service litigation and bill decisions (2026-10-12)

`servicePhase` records actual consular refusals, a HUD complaint/provider criterion, and an investigated wage shortfall before a court matter is created. `fair-housing-court.ts` and `wage-hour-court.ts` use the general proceeding/effect path, preserve dated source evidence and return scoped remedies to the original service record. The private applicant's application and the investigated worker's wages remain distinct from aggregate nationwide benefit estimates. These lower-court matters remain visible on the docket without manufacturing a presidential decision or Supreme Court roll call.

`surveillancePackets` can record an unauthorized domestic interception by the agency. `oversight.ts` exposes that existing log; `surveillance-court.ts` then admits the aggrieved person's statutory damages action. This is distinct from the collection boundary, which refuses unsupported presidential domestic surveillance. Liability does not widen lawful collection authority. Agency compliance after judgment is a separate recorded action.

`scenario-bill.ts` selects an available real markup/conference action and freezes its bill through the existing content/case snapshot path. The content audit requires the typed gate, cast and executable response to agree. `conversation-report-actions.ts` uses the same proposal/conference resolver as the direct Congress desk, both for projection and commitment. The liveness check rejects a changed stage or text; case withdrawal prevents an obsolete choice from blocking Continue or charging inaction. No parallel bill mutation or legislative authority is added.


`selectCurrentLaws` is the continuing-provision reader; `selectLaws` is the immutable historical statute book. Housing repeal/renewal/replacement pin their predecessor at introduction and use existing chamber/presentment/enactment paths. `lawOffsetForBill` reads the enacted succession/provision for recurring fiscal and resting effects. Construction is a liability exception: the appropriation phase stops new obligations after authority ends but pays existing commitments, and the fiscal reader retains that cash outside the current-law filter. Neither a renewal nor a stale rival revision can replay the original one-off benefit.


Stafford relief reuses `obligationPhase` for the dated governor request, the state effect for a distinct presidential major-disaster declaration, and `enactBill` for the fixed recovery appropriation shared by signed, unsigned and override routes. `reliefOrderSubjects` validates the actual live disaster, request/declaration lineage, operative bill, funding horizon and remaining balance. `signOrder` records the allocation without a second fiscal debit. Report projection and commit use that same subject/resolver; missing authority is a disabled response with an explicit reason. For newly stamped recovery authority, `pricedOffset` annualizes the fixed nominal ceiling over the original funding span and converts that cash to the current GDP share used by the fiscal reader. GDP growth cannot expand the appropriation. Legacy laws keep their existing fiscal flows but cannot authorize a new allocation without known nominal authority.


`order-lifecycle.ts` routes both presidential rescission and recorded statutory expiry through `orderRescission` plus the existing rule-cancellation and contribution-remedy paths. `orderExpiryPhase` runs in the ordinary turn phases; the Order document exposes the same `rescindOrder` resolver as a reviewed player action. This ends future authority while retaining the signed record, court injury, sunk payments and debt. Rule withdrawal distinguishes not-yet-effective cancellation from an effective rule's rescission. Construction uses the same ended-order check to stop new obligations while paying liabilities already incurred.


Cabinet contract steering uses `cabinet-graft.ts` in the existing agenda week. It reads enacted purchasing exposure from the budget and `accountDepartments`, records the original actor and opportunity in existing causal provenance, and changes institution integrity through the ordinary effect gate. Its annual role identity prevents replay or replacement from repeating the same opportunity. `inspectors-general.ts` opens a generic purchasing inquiry after a pressure-dependent delay and its report later names the actor from the stored record; `OversightMatter.causeId` and scandal attribution preserve the original actor. Player history readers use `graftDisclosed` before exposing rows, links or counts, and omit private willingness terms even when a public finding permits the original act to be read. The weekly Digest preserves its existing ledger date windows but groups the rendered rows by the same disclosed actor classification as the Record, so an official’s disclosed act belongs to the administration without changing its stored causal actor or exposing it earlier. The exposure proxy does not imply a contract ledger or measure dollars stolen.

Order expiry is also checked after `advanceOneDay` steps the date, before its daily phases, and after the weekly `advanceTurn` moves the date, before returning player state. Daily play therefore ends the recorded ninety-day authority on day ninety. The direct weekly batch helper evaluates a week at its opening date and cannot partition within-week effects across that boundary; its returned state is ended. Service production excludes rescinded entry orders, so a historical proclamation cannot create fresh visa refusals.


### Institutional binding and bounded collection

Content's vacancy binder selects a real vacancy instance and two actual eligible people, freezing their public records; commit calls the existing nomination resolver. Conference binding reads two differing passed chamber texts from one live bill and calls the existing recommendation resolver. Both follow the existing scenario occurrence, attention, revalidation and history path; a stale subject is explicitly withdrawn without an invented inaction consequence. A procedural choice is not enacted policy.

Collection reports are immutable issued products. The candidate pool reads existing pursuits, force/support commitments, conditions and public state requests; discipline access is applied before a bounded, seeded rotation. A later collection cycle can reveal a different eligible fact, while re-opening a past report cannot replace its contents. Selected-finding receipts and recipient cooperation consumers read only the disclosed snapshot. Shared organization membership supplies no private-government access.

Regulatory review identifies an existing live rule and records a finding rather than silently repealing it. Offshore planning identifies a sourced BOEM area and expires with the applicable program. Neither writes an immediate national growth/import benefit. Actual purchasing, lease award, permitting and production require their own authority, transactions and delivery; the order's signature is not evidence they occurred.


`offshore-leasing.ts` implements the follow-through from an operative planning rule: funded Interior review, environmental analysis, consultation, notices, seeded bids, fair-value review, an explicit accept/decline decision, and separate exploration-plan review. `offshoreLeaseSaleAction` and `offshoreLeaseSalePhase` use the existing effect/apply path; the Order document reads those same records/actions. Shutdown, low delivery capacity and a temporary court stay pause unfinished work; expired program authority and invalidated rules have distinct terminal consequences. The award's rights remain after the planning order ends. The sale-review obligation consumes existing discretionary authority once. A dated bid bonus enters the national books once via `pendingOffshoreLeaseBonus` and persisted receipt IDs, reducing borrowing without creating a new spending appropriation. This bounded modeled tract does not assert actual BOEM bids, production or a national import benefit.


`clean-power-purchasing.ts` separates direction of a purchasing rule from an actual federal portfolio obligation and delivered electricity. `buyFederalCleanPower` validates the operative rule, modeled supply headroom and existing `obligationAvailability`, then consumes $94 million of discretionary authority once. Weekly delivery records MWh across the two-year portfolio; it does not manufacture a national generation or import change. Contractual rights continue after the directing order ends. The energy pledge requires delivery plus the independently measured import result. The revised signatures for single-site protection, single-rule review, lease planning and clean-power direction no longer retain the obsolete GDP-scaled implementation charges; later funded transactions carry their own identified obligations.

`blocked-assets.ts` uses actual bilateral import exposure as the eligibility basis for one explicitly modeled settlement account. The signed external-threat order records the account and a uniquely linked existing `asset-restriction` trade measure, whose volume reader provides the operational restriction. `order-lifecycle.ts` releases that exact block on rescission; `blockedAssetPhase` also observes the matching emergency and final judicial result. Lifting a block retains ownership/history and does not lift another measure. This is a bounded settlement-exposure abstraction, not an asset-balance or confiscation simulation.


Congress does not require a presidential recommendation to finish a conference. `advanceBills` permits a response window, then `conferenceWithoutRecommendation` selects one of the two passed texts by the stronger weakest-chamber vote margin, using a seeded tie break. A recorded conference beat preserves this decision; a later sitting holds separate House and Senate votes. `conferenceRecommendationDeadline` derives the last response date from the same sitting schedule. Content freezes that date on `FrozenBill`, refuses later casts and caps both the authored case clock and dossier closing date. The deadline precedes the autonomous sitting because daily case defaults run before the date advances into the weekly legislative phase. This avoids allowing the player to veto Congress by withholding a recommendation or silently shortening an offered response window.


`order-authority.ts` is the shared prospective-authority reader for pending/effective orders under court review. An adverse lower order judgment pauses future operation immediately. `answerLowerOrderReview` closes the dated response and records acceptance, review, or review with a stay application. The later court phase decides the stay independently; only a grant suspends that judgment. Services, rulemaking, offshore work, clean-power awards, construction, defense priority and linked blocked-property trade volume consult that authority. Stored trade-measure identity survives a temporary pause. Restoration cannot override an independent case, statutory end or rescission; historical delivery, liability and rights are retained. The Court file distinguishes a request, the court's decision and the order's present authority without publishing internal odds.

Defense priority extends the existing munitions order book instead of creating a second procurement ledger. The named modeled supplier is attached to an outstanding precision-fires replenishment entry. While authority permits, bounded additional line capacity works down that entry; an overdue residual remains owed. A court limit or rescission ends prospective priority, preserving delivered stock and outstanding obligations. No GDP-scaled signature price or national growth/readiness signature remains. This does not yet model a new contract's cash price or physical quantity.

Clean-power cash follows recorded delivery, with no payment during a supply delay. Its cumulative paid memo is separate from the ordinary annualized discretionary outlay, avoiding a second national-books debit. Legacy purchases establish a tracking baseline at previously delivered MWh rather than inventing prior cash. Order documents distinguish original obligation, recorded payments, unknown legacy history and delivered electricity. Federal Books consumes `FiscalStatement.cleanPowerPaymentsRecorded` as a cumulative memo beside the annual revenue/outlay tables, explicitly retaining unknown earlier legacy payments.

The lower-bench extension reuses the institution, person, nomination and judicial effect owners. Filing selects a geographically appropriate district forum from dated aggregate source rows. District merits retain the legal-ground calculation with a small, explicitly modeled appointer-mix adjustment in the order's economic direction; neutral policy direction adds none. A circuit appeal assigns three eligible occupied seats, materializing fictional people only when required for the case and recording prior-government-advice recusals against that order. Their individual legal thresholds read one shared factual draw. A vacant, dead, displaced or recused judge cannot participate; two remaining assigned judges may rule only if unanimous, and a split seeks a replacement rather than becoming an upheld judgment. The circuit holding supersedes the district holding for prospective order authority, while both remain historical records. Supreme discretionary review then reads the circuit posture. The case file shows assignment, participation and recorded recusals; missing panel history on older cases remains missing. Other recusal grounds and broader lower-court lifecycle details remain pending.

`court-calendar.ts` schedules ordinary Supreme arguments in the October term and a late-June opinion target; emergency stay applications keep their own clock. A lack of quorum can carry the case rather than fabricate a judgment. `solicitor-general.ts` keeps a bounded counsel-conduct assessment through the existing judiciary effect path. Ordinary litigation wins or losses do not themselves change it. Recorded overreach and correction affect consideration of review/stay, while case votes continue to depend on legal merits and judge characteristics. Screens show qualitative counsel assessment and dated conduct, not internal odds.

`border-incidents.ts` runs inside the existing world-event phase and effect fold. It separates uncertain exchanges at actual land borders from deliberate hostile actions. The opening packet carries no government initiator, betrayal charge or invented casualty count. Later evidence may support inadvertence or leave the incident unresolved; any tension relief must be attributable to the incident, and neither waiting nor a common enemy generates trust. Report sharing copies the assessment already issued, without a second diplomatic reward for the same public evidence. The producer includes tense adversarial borders and suppresses a deliberate clash on the same pair in that week. Each direction records its pre-incident pressure and actual clamped additions; subsequent relief cannot remove more than that remaining attributable increment. These bounds and rival-border behaviors have source-level regression proof and await integrated validation.

Defense inspector investigations pin the particular recorded NSA interception at opening in `OversightMatter.surveillanceIncidentId`. `oversightRipeningPhase` requires the same identity before publishing that collection log and exposing the incident for its court consumer. Shared department and opening chronology alone are insufficient; unrelated audits retain their own findings. Missing legacy links are not reconstructed.

## Scenario continuity and accounting flow (2026-10-14)

`scenario-subjects.ts` extends the content entity registry and reads existing case bindings, governors and institution definitions. It resolves identity before text rendering. `scenario-cast.ts` passes the resolved state/city into prose tokens, state effects, script subjects and locales; `scenario-cases.ts` freezes that script and a typed story binding on the same presidential case. Closed cases retain identities; inherited callbacks reuse them. The existing causal graph receives true flag gates and subject-origin case event IDs. `rememberReply` refreshes causal marks after adding authored memory, so every commit consumer can resolve a later callback.

Cost draws share `engine/scenario-cost-draw.ts`, preserving the original bounded distribution. Content attaches an estimate to authored choice borrowing; `settleScenarioAccounting` runs only on successful position commit and converts it to a separate booked draw using `bookUsDebt` precision. The ordinary apply path books it and retains the estimate/actual receipt on the ledger. Preview does not settle or consume randomness. Record detail reads receipts and subject identities, while the existing timeline query/subject filter walks all decisions naming that subject. No runtime generation, new dependency, separate save store or parallel history is introduced.

The existing city bake also emits a context-only sourced settlement table. Settlement resolution shares the fit/recency state draw and freezes the actual point in the case. It never fabricates a town for a national condition or treats locality population as production capacity. Explicit road-border and port requirements use separately evidenced flags. The prior economic-city table is unchanged.

`municipal-offices.ts` uses the existing fictional person generator, a stable city-based identity, and the ordinary person-create effect. Casting remains pure; case raise materializes the official, and save/dossier readers use that same person. The mayor role is on-demand rather than part of the founding slate. Governor/member/mayor letters and readouts bind to the existing current official, while `inherit` retains a historical subject. Subject history uses native person/institution ids across unrelated cases and retains separate prose-token aliases. This extends the case/ledger/Record owners rather than creating a parallel continuity store.

The existing dataset place resolver now resolves settlement context as well as cities/states/nations. Content datelines and Record event pins share its coordinates/name; the Record does not silently substitute a state metro for a sourced settlement. Its existing state camera/filter remains intact. Explicit contradictory locality targets fail closed.

Instrument and join stamping append declared subject identities to the resolver’s own entity list while preserving its ordering and confidentiality. Resolver provenance must not overwrite those identities; position, instrument and join records share the same subject extraction.
