A real instrument-reply regression checks the ledger entity as well as the retained subject metadata.

### Guard request scope and units

A governor-consent packet in `guard-federalization.ts` charges ten USD millions for support coordination. It previously used a raw-dollar magnitude as a USD-million treasury delta. The corrected packet leaves military command/deployments unchanged; visible copy distinguishes consent from federalization. Domestic force legality requires its own implemented predicates and cannot be inferred from an emergency condition or governor forecast.

### Domestic force: missions, orders and court orders (2026-10-25)

This replaces the October 15 boundary, which refused generic agent surges, Guard federalization and active-duty troops. The acts now open, each on the predicate its statute names (DECISIONS §6.22). Nothing here is inferred from a declaration, a protest or a foreign-war flag.

**The files.** `locale-acts.ts` is the catalogue (`LOCALE_ACTS`, 13 ids, with `group`, `occasion`, `predicate`, `authority`, `orderId`, `mission`, `commitment`, `governor`, `mark` and the tiered Insurrection Act values). `locale-missions.ts` holds the predicates and what a mission is worth to the unrest it answers; it imports no module that computes a turn, so `conditions.ts` can read it. `locale-act-effects.ts` holds `takeLocaleAct`, `takeHomeAct` and `localeActProjection` (the read a desk shows before the act is taken, out of the same reads the act takes). `locale-mission-orders.ts` holds `LOCALE_MISSION_ORDERS`, spliced into `orders.ts`'s lookup but kept out of `EXECUTIVE_ORDERS` so the policy desk and `availableOrders` never see them. Money is charged by the act, not the order, so a signature cannot bill a mission twice.

**One packet per act.** `takeLocaleAct` checks availability (`localeAvailability`: state, boundary, declaration, occasion, governor assent, the statutory predicate, one investigative priority per state and week, the mission order's own gate, funding), then signs the mission order for the state through `signOrder` with a `locale-mission` subject, carries its counsel and provenance, issues the directive for a priority, opens the commitment for troops and the Guard, and adds the price, the governor's step and mark, opinion, stability, readiness, the answered claim, the protest move and the coercion outcome. The order's own refusal text comes from `missionOrderRefusal`, so a direct signature cannot skip the predicate.

**Predicates.**
- `insurrectionGrounds` reads each section off this week's record: §251 when the governor assents and unrest reaching the state is at `INSURRECTION_SEVERITY.request` (0.4); §252 when unrest placed in the state is at `OBSTRUCTION_STILL` (0.3, below the 0.5 the suit needs, because the mission that made the suit possible also eases the unrest while the court decides) and `obstructed` holds (a granted or narrowed `injunction-enforcement` order at least `OBSTRUCTION_WEEKS` = 1 old and no `answered:relief:unrest` mark since); §253 when placed unrest is at `.rights` (0.6), the governor does not assent and `stateProtecting` is false (no answer within `ANSWER_ATTENTION_TURNS`, 6). `unrestReaching` takes placed unrest first and falls back to national unrest; only §251 reads the fallback.
- `liveProclamation` finds the state's `order-insurrection-proclamation` record issued an earlier week, inside `PROCLAMATION_VALID_TURNS` (4), still operative. The troops act reads its ground, which picks `order-insurrection-request` (§251) or `order-insurrection-enforcement` (§§252-253) and the tier's standing cost, governor step, mark and stated mission. Moving the force needs a new act: the commitment is bound to that state and order.
- `federalServiceGround` returns `invasion` (an active conflict with the founding nation as defender), `rebellion` (`insurgencyPressure` at `REBELLION_INSURGENCY_FLOOR`, 0.2) or `regular-forces` (`regularForcesUnable`: a live proclamation, unrest reaching the state at 0.5 or worse, and a troops commitment started at least `REGULAR_FORCES_WEEKS` (2) earlier under an operative order). `stackedMission` caps the state at one federalize and one troops mission and refuses federalizing in the week troops were ordered.

**Attention.** `missionAttention` reads live mission order records, live `authorizedBy` commitments and carried `investigative-priority:<state>` flags. A placed condition takes the heaviest mission in its state; national unrest takes each state's weight times its population share, capped at 1. `conditionAttention` takes `Math.max` of that against its existing terms, so stacking missions adds cost and risk, not attention. `MISSION_ATTENTION` holds attention and weeks for property-surge, task-force, federalize, troops and priority; `MISSION_WEEKS` holds the term of every mission, including the immigration operation, which eases nothing but whose weeks a court reads as the federal law it enforces. `liveLocaleMissions` lists the missions still inside their window with whether each order operates.

**Suits and the interim ruling.** A mission order is a decree already on the street, so the first court step against it is an injunction, not a stay of a rule. In `judicial-review.ts`'s `interim` stage `missionInterimRelief` (`mission-interim-relief.ts`) draws against `merits × (0.5 + 0.5 × harm)`, plus `STATE_PLAINTIFF_RELIEF` where a state leads the suit and its governor did not assent, bounded to 0.05 to 0.9. `INTERIM_HARM` ranks the acts by harm (troops and the Guard highest, a task force the governor invited lowest). A granted ruling sets `CourtProceeding.interim` to `"order-enjoined"`; `isOrderCourtSuspended` treats that as suspended, so `prospectiveOrderAuthority` is not `operative`, the commitment's `expired` fires and the force stands down. The weights are game assumptions, not observed grant rates. An enjoined proclamation does not recall troops already sent.

**Court orders the administration seeks.** `court-orders.ts` holds the application and `court-order-records.ts` the record. `requestCourtOrder` issues a Justice directive keyed `court:<kind>:<state>`; only a carried instruction files. The odds come from `courtOrderEvidence` (FBI capability, the kind's own predicate, a carried priority, collection already run under an order) and, for the injunction, the district bench; the FISA approval and narrowing rates are the 2025 Foreign Intelligence Surveillance Court figures, and every other number is a planning value. The record is turn-stamped flags, so no save field is added: filing writes `court-application:<kind>:<state>:g<pct>:n<pct>` (the odds shown, as whole percents, never the answer); on the week before the court answers, `courtOrderRulingEffects` (run from `judicial-review.ts`) draws `stream("court-order", kind, state, appliedTurn)` against those odds and writes `court-order-<outcome>:<kind>:<state>`, stamped with the week the court gives it. Nothing that reads raw flags can see an outcome earlier. `courtOrderRecords` derives `pending`, `granted`, `narrowed`, `denied` or `expired` from the two flags and the kind's term; a denial starts a `DENIAL_COOLDOWN_WEEKS` (4) cool-down, and a sealed denial can leak (`LEAK_CHANCE`), which sets a `scandal:` flag and costs standing. `liveCourtOrder` is what `surveil-under-order` (a FISA or Title III order) and the §252 ground (an injunction) read. `courtOrderLosses` feeds `legal-exposure.ts`'s loss count with public injunctions that were denied or narrowed.

**Investigative priority.** `priority` issues an `INST-DOJ` `enforcement-posture` directive keyed by the state, so two states in one week are both carried and the same state twice is refused; a carried one writes `investigative-priority:<state>` with its turn marker. Court applications use their own key prefix and do not collide with it.

**Surveillance.** `surveil` is refused in every case (50 U.S.C. §1812; Executive Order 12333 §2.3). `surveil-under-order` requires `liveCourtOrder(..., SURVEILLANCE_ORDER_KINDS)`, leaves a `surveilled-under-order:<state>` flag that counts as evidence for the next application for `SURVEILLANCE_EVIDENCE_WEEKS` (8), and runs its coercion odds at `NARROWED_ORDER_ODDS` (1.5) times the base when the order was narrowed.

**Known simplifications (accepted).** The troops act stands in for the Insurrection Act's own call on the militia; invasion is read off any war the United States defends; the §252 predicate is a game reading of "impracticable by the ordinary course of judicial proceedings"; court odds and the injunction term, other than the FISA figures, are planning values.
